- **Citation:** typescript-eslint `packages/eslint-plugin/docs/rules/no-use-before-define.mdx` and `src/rules/no-use-before-define.ts` at `v8.72.0`; issue #10389 (mojito317, 25 Nov 2024, open, accepting prs). https://typescript-eslint.io/rules/no-use-before-define
- **What it says:** the docs are short: "It adds support for `type`, `interface` and `enum` declarations." Defaults in the source: `functions: true`, `classes: true`, `variables: true`, `enums: true`, `typedefs: true`, `ignoreTypeReferences: true`, `allowNamedExports: false`. The string option `"nofunc"`: "Broadly set functions and allowNamedExports to false." How they apply (source): `functions` covers only function declarations (`DefinitionType.FunctionName`), so a `const` arrow is governed by `variables`; `variables: false` ignores only uses from an inner function scope ("Checks whether or not a given variable is a variable declaration" / "in an upper function scope."); same-scope uses are always reported. The docs say nothing about TypeScript making the rule redundant and have no "When Not To Use It" section. In #10389, kirkwaiblinger: "I couldn't get any tests in our repo to fail" / "by forcing the typedefs option to be true/false"; StyleShit (Aug 2026): "these are 2 completely conflicting options, we need to remove one of them".
- **What the defaults mean for Ultracite:** with every rule on at "error" and no options, function declarations used above their definition are reported (so hoisting is no excuse), uses inside later-running functions are reported, and type references are not checked (`ignoreTypeReferences: true` wins over `typedefs: true`).
- **Caveat:** the schema's wording is inconsistent: `functions` is described as "Whether to ignore references to function declarations." while `true` means report them; `enums` is "Whether to check references to enums." ESLint 10.13.0's core rule now has the same TypeScript options (`dialects` JavaScript and TypeScript) and the same defaults.
- **For the post:** with defaults, the rule enforces source order, not runtime safety: it flags safe code (2.17) to catch the cases `tsc` misses.
- **Text:** `scratchpad/func-style-lit/B/src/typescript-eslint/docs-no-use-before-define@v8.72.0.mdx`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/B/src/typescript-eslint/src-no-use-before-define@v8.72.0.ts`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/B/text/tseslint-issue-10389*.json`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/B/src/eslint/no-use-before-define@v10.13.0.js`

### 2.16 The extension's history: added in 2017, recommended until 2020, dropped because "generally caught by typescript". VERIFIED (PRs, issues, configs at tags)

- **Citation:** j-f1, "no-use-before-define," nzakas/eslint-plugin-typescript (now bradzacher/eslint-plugin-typescript, archived) PR #34 (opened 6 Aug 2017, merged 9 Aug 2017, released in 0.4.0); typescript-eslint `recommended.json` at `v1.13.0` and `v2.0.0`, `recommended.ts` at `v3.0.0`; issue #651 (jacobrask, 26 Jun 2019); issue #1423, "Changes to the `recommended` sets for 3.0.0" (bradzacher, 10 Jan 2020); PR #2001 (bradzacher, 10 May 2020); issue #11278 (6 Jun 2025, working as intended); issue #11014 (JoshuaKGoldberg, 31 Mar 2025, open). https://github.com/typescript-eslint/typescript-eslint/issues/1423
- **What it says:**
  - PR #34 ports the core rule "with a new `typedefs` option to ignore errors coming from `type` declarations."
  - `recommended` at v1.13.0 and v2.0.0 set `@typescript-eslint/no-use-before-define` to "error"; v3.0.0 doesn't list it. PR #2001 ("update recommended sets", "Fixes #1423") removes it.
  - #651, phaux (24 Jul 2019) turned it off and would "always define function _after_ all it's uses" (sic). bradzacher: "It's stylistic, but everyone seems to be okay with it." "Might be worth removing the rule because the errors that should be caught" / "by this should be caught by typescript instead."
  - #1423: "The errors caught by this rule are generally caught by typescript." "There's a few edge cases, but by-and-large, it's covered." "It also relies upon scope analysis, which is currently in a bad state." "Verdict: remove."
  - #11278 (2025), bradzacher, on a user who wanted a later `const` read inside a function allowed: "The rule errors because it's easy to write broken code like this." The example calls the function before the `const`: "This code will crash."
  - #11014 lists `no-use-before-define` among extension rules "being replaced with core ESLint rules" and proposes deprecating them.
- **Caveat:** the #1423 body was edited after posting (it links #1856, a later issue), so the wording may postdate 10 Jan 2020. The "edge cases" in #1423 are exactly the call-through cases 2.13 measures; they aren't rare by construction.
- **For the post:** the maintainers removed the rule from `recommended` on the grounds that TypeScript covers it, and five years later defended its strictness with the case TypeScript misses. Both are true; 2.13 shows the size of the gap.
- **Text:** `scratchpad/func-style-lit/B/text/eslint-plugin-typescript-pr-34*.json`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/B/src/typescript-eslint/recommended@v1.13.0.json`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/B/src/typescript-eslint/recommended@v2.0.0.json`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/B/src/typescript-eslint/recommended@v3.0.0.ts`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/B/text/tseslint-issue-651*.json`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/B/text/tseslint-issue-1423*.json`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/B/text/tseslint-pr-2001.json`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/B/text/tseslint-issue-11278*.json`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/B/text/tseslint-issue-11014*.json`

### 2.17 Lint run: the default options catch 8 of the 10 crashes and flag 5 safe cases; no option separates them. VERIFIED (local run: ESLint 10.13.0 with typescript-eslint 8.72.0, Oxlint 1.89.0)

- **Citation:** the 20 cases of 2.13, linted with only `@typescript-eslint/no-use-before-define` under three option sets, and with Oxlint 1.89.0 `no-use-before-define` (defaults), 29 Oct 2026. `scratchpad/func-style-lit/B/check/lint/`.
- **What it shows:** defaults flag 8 of the 10 crashing cases (all but the two that cross modules, t5 and `cycle-const`) and 6 of the 10 non-crashing ones; 5 of those 6 are safe code (a3, a6, a10, t2, t3; a11 is a real `var` bug). `{ variables: false }` flags 4 of 10 crashes (a1, a7 via the declaration only, a9, t1), about what `tsc` already catches, and 3 non-crashing cases. `{ functions: false }` keeps all 8 crash detections and drops the two function-declaration false positives (a6, t3). Oxlint's diagnostics on the `.ts` files match typescript-eslint's defaults line for line (16 each).
- **Caveat:** same small, hand-made sample as 2.13. No option can separate t2 (safe) from t4 (crash): both are a use of a later `const` inside a function, and only call order differs.
- **For the post:** the rule's value over `tsc` is exactly the call-through cases, bought by flagging some safe orderings. With `const` functions you can't use `functions: false` to drop the false positives, because the exemption only applies to declarations.
- **Text:** `scratchpad/func-style-lit/B/check/RESULTS.txt`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/B/check/lint/eslint-tseslint-defaults.out`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/B/check/lint/eslint-tseslint-variables-false.out`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/B/check/lint/eslint-tseslint-functions-false.out`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/B/check/lint/oxlint-defaults-ts-only.out`

### 2.18 typescript-eslint's shared configs: only `all` turns on `no-use-before-define`; none has a function-style rule. VERIFIED (config sources and docs at `v8.72.0`)

- **Citation:** typescript-eslint `packages/eslint-plugin/src/configs/flat/{recommended,strict,stylistic,stylistic-type-checked,strict-type-checked,recommended-type-checked,all}.ts`, `src/configs/eslint-recommended-raw.ts` and `docs/users/Shared_Configurations.mdx` at `v8.72.0`. https://typescript-eslint.io/users/configs
- **What it says:** `no-use-before-define` appears only in `all` (base "off", extension "error"). `stylistic` and `stylistic-type-checked` include `prefer-function-type`; `explicit-function-return-type`, `explicit-module-boundary-types` and `method-signature-style` are only in `all`. `eslint-recommended` "disables core ESLint rules that are already checked by the TypeScript compiler" and doesn't mention `no-use-before-define` (which isn't in `eslint:recommended`). On `all`: "We do not recommend TypeScript projects extend from `plugin:@typescript-eslint/all`." "Many rules conflict with each other and/or are intended to be configured per-project." On `no-undef`, the FAQ goes further: "We strongly recommend that you do not use the `no-undef` lint rule" / "on TypeScript projects."
- **Caveat:** no shared config contains anything like `func-style`; neither the base rule nor an extension exists in typescript-eslint.
- **For the post:** Ultracite's ESLint preset enables every non-deprecated typescript-eslint rule at "error", which is close to the `all` config the project warns against. That's a deliberate strictness choice, but the post shouldn't describe `no-use-before-define` as a typescript-eslint recommendation.
- **Text:** `scratchpad/func-style-lit/B/src/typescript-eslint/config-flat-*.ts`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/B/src/typescript-eslint/config-eslint-recommended-raw@v8.72.0.ts`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/B/src/typescript-eslint/docs/docs-users-Shared_Configurations@v8.72.0.mdx`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/B/src/typescript-eslint/docs/docs-troubleshooting-faqs-ESLint@v8.72.0.mdx`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/B/src/ultracite/eslint-core-rules-typescript.mjs`

### 2.19 Related typescript-eslint rules: `allowTypedFunctionExpressions` exists because typing through the variable is common. VERIFIED (docs and source at `v8.72.0`, issue #651, TS 2.6 notes); Vendor (Microsoft) for the TS 2.6 notes

- **Citation:** typescript-eslint docs and source for `explicit-function-return-type`, `explicit-module-boundary-types`, `prefer-function-type`, `method-signature-style` at `v8.72.0`; issue #651 (2019); TypeScript 2.6 release notes at `205122bb`. https://typescript-eslint.io/rules/explicit-function-return-type
- **What it says:** `allowTypedFunctionExpressions` (default `true` in both return-type rules): "Whether to ignore type annotations on the variable of function expressions." Its docs show `let arrowFn: FuncType = () => 'test';` as correct. In 2019 bradzacher wrote of the return-type rule: "We need to turn allowTypedFunctionExpressions on by default." `prefer-function-type`: "The function type form is generally preferred when possible for being more succinct." `method-signature-style` (default `"property"`): "methods are always bivariant in their arguments, while function properties are contravariant." TypeScript 2.6: "The stricter checking applies to all function types," / "_except_ those originating in method or constructor declarations."
- **Caveat:** Ultracite turns `explicit-function-return-type` and `explicit-module-boundary-types` off and `method-signature-style` and `prefer-function-type` on (Oxlint and ESLint). Bivariance is about method signatures versus function-typed properties; function declarations and `const` arrows are both function types and are checked the same way under `strictFunctionTypes`.
- **For the post:** the return-type rules exempt typed variables by default because so much code types functions that way, which supports claim (2) as a description of practice. Variance differences belong to methods versus properties, not to declaration versus `const`.
- **Text:** `scratchpad/func-style-lit/B/src/typescript-eslint/docs-explicit-function-return-type@v8.72.0.mdx`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/B/src/typescript-eslint/src-explicit-function-return-type@v8.72.0.ts`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/B/src/typescript-eslint/docs-prefer-function-type@v8.72.0.mdx`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/B/src/typescript-eslint/docs-method-signature-style@v8.72.0.mdx`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/B/text/tseslint-issue-651-comments.json`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/B/src/ts-website/release-notes-TypeScript_2.6.md@205122bb.md`

### 2.20 Maintainers on `func-style` in TypeScript: "the declaration style doesn't matter", and ESLint's 2025 `allowTypeAnnotation`. ANECDOTAL (maintainer comments); VERIFIED (ESLint PR, source and docs at `v10.13.0`)

- **Citation:** typescript-eslint issue #8488, "Base rule extension: [func-style] Allow type-annotated variable declaration" (remcohaszing, 16 Feb 2024, closed wontfix 25 Aug 2024); eslint/eslint #18842 (NatoBoram, 2 Sep 2024, closed 8 Oct 2024), #19719 (remcohaszing, 10 May 2025, accepted), PR #19754 "feat: add allowTypeAnnotation to func-style" (sethamus, 22–28 May 2025); ESLint `docs/src/rules/func-style.md` at `v10.13.0`. https://github.com/typescript-eslint/typescript-eslint/issues/8488
- **What it says:**
  - bradzacher (#8488, 21 Feb 2024): "My 2c has always been that the declaration style doesn't matter" / "and linting it doesn't meaningfully improve your codebase." "If anything I'd consider expressions/arrows safer because they aren't hoisted" / "and thus aren't subject to the same hoisting unsafety as declarations." On usage: "Based on some quick, naive searches `func-style` is turned on" / "1/10th as much as `no-unused-vars`."
  - auvred (a declarations user): when a function must implement a type, "typed function expressions are the only way to do it." Josh-Cena's style: "Use function declarations to bind functions to identifiers." and "If the function needs a type annotation, use an arrow function."
  - ESLint #18842: mdjermanovic called it "technically a bug in the `func-style` rule when used with TypeScript code" "because it suggests something that cannot be done." nzakas suggested "just not to use the `func-style` rule, or else use a disable directive". The TSC closed it: "it has been decided that to close this issue" / "as it is more of an edge case."
  - #19719 (2025): nzakas, "I believe the intent here is to make `func-style` TypeScript-aware," and fasttime called `const fn: CustomFunction = (...) => {...}` "the idiomatic and most concise way to ensure that a function `fn` satisfies" its signature. PR #19754 added `allowTypeAnnotation`, which "allows the use of function expressions and arrow functions" / "when the variable declaration has type annotation, regardless of the" `allowArrowFunctions` option, only in "declaration" mode.
- **Caveat:** personal views of maintainers and one usage estimate from unspecified searches. The `allowTypeAnnotation` option is for declaration-preferring configs; it says nothing about Ultracite's "expression" setting.
- **For the post:** both linters' maintainers treat "typed through the variable" as the one place a `const` is needed, not as a reason to make every function a `const`. bradzacher's view that arrows are safer because they aren't hoisted is the clearest maintainer statement for claim (3), and 2.13 shows its limits.
- **Text:** `scratchpad/func-style-lit/B/text/typescript-eslint-typescript-eslint-issues-8488.json`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/B/text/typescript-eslint-typescript-eslint-issues-8488-comments.json`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/B/text/eslint-eslint-issues-18842*.json`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/B/text/eslint-issue-19719*.json`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/B/text/eslint-pr-19754.json`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/B/src/eslint/docs-func-style@v10.13.0.md`

### 2.21 The TypeScript compiler's own code: declarations allowed by lint, about 2,456 to 6 in `checker.ts`. VERIFIED (config, local rule and source at `v6.0.3`); Vendor (Microsoft)

- **Citation:** microsoft/TypeScript `eslint.config.mjs` and `scripts/eslint/rules/only-arrow-functions.cjs` at `v6.0.3`; `src/compiler/checker.ts` at `v6.0.3`. https://github.com/microsoft/TypeScript/blob/v6.0.3/eslint.config.mjs
- **What it says:** the repo has no `func-style`; it runs a local rule, `local/only-arrow-functions`, described as "Disallows traditional (non-arrow) function expressions." and configured with `allowNamedFunctions: true` and `allowDeclarations: true`, so declarations are allowed and anonymous `function` expressions are not. A line-start regex count over `checker.ts` finds 2,456 function declarations, 6 `const`/`let` arrow bindings and 0 `const`/`let` function expressions (`scratchpad/func-style-lit/B/check/typescript-checker-function-counts.txt`). The use-before-declaration check itself (2.12) calls a nested helper, `isUsedInFunctionOrInstanceProperty`, about 110 lines above that helper's declaration.
- **Caveat:** the count is approximate (single-line heads only) and covers one file. The compiler is a special code base (performance-tuned, written before many modern conventions); it's an example, not evidence of what's best.
- **For the post:** the team that designed TypeScript's types writes its compiler almost entirely in function declarations and relies on hoisting inside functions, while linting against anonymous function expressions.
- **Text:** `scratchpad/func-style-lit/B/src/typescript/eslint.config@v6.0.3.mjs`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/B/src/typescript/only-arrow-functions@v6.0.3.cjs`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/B/src/typescript/compiler/checker@v6.0.3.ts`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/B/check/typescript-checker-function-counts.txt`

## 3. The rules: where `func-style`, `no-use-before-define` and their neighbours came from

Items 3.1–3.3 are the pre-ESLint history (JSLint, JSHint and the hoisting article both tools cite); 3.4–3.6 are ESLint's `no-use-before-define`; 3.7–3.10 are ESLint's `func-style`; 3.11–3.14 are the neighbouring ESLint rules (`func-names`, `func-name-matching`, `prefer-arrow-callback`, `arrow-body-style`, `no-inner-declarations`, `no-func-assign`); 3.15–3.16 are Oxlint's ports; 3.17–3.20 are Biome; 3.21–3.24 are the plugin rules (`react/function-component-definition`, `unicorn/consistent-function-scoping`, `antfu/top-level-function`, the two prefer-arrow plugins); 3.25 is the TypeScript constraint that several of these rules carve exceptions for; 3.26–3.27 are my local runs of all three linters on the same files. Rule docs and sources were downloaded at the pinned tags (ESLint `v10.13.0`, Oxlint `oxlint_v1.89.0` = `07ed78f4`, Biome `@biomejs/biome@2.6.3` = `c5c3383d`, eslint-plugin-react `v7.37.5`, eslint-plugin-unicorn `v79.0.0` = `8425e259`, eslint-plugin-antfu `v3.3.1`, JSHint `2.13.6`, JSLint at `6610addf` (2011) and `dfee16b5` (Oct 2026)) into `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/src/`. Issues and PRs were read with read-only `gh api` and saved as text in `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/text/gh/` (file names `<owner>_<repo>-<number>.txt`; commit lists `commits-*.tsv`). Local runs are in `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/check/`. All searches and downloads were done on 29 Oct 2026.

### 3.1 JSLint: "'{a}' was used before it was defined." (by 2011), still JSLint's wording in 2026. VERIFIED (source and docs at pinned commits)

- **Citation:** Douglas Crockford, `jslint.js` dated 2011-04-14 and `lint.html`, jslint-org/jslint commit `6610addf` (16 Apr 2011, the oldest commit holding `jslint.js`); current `jslint.mjs` on the `beta` branch at `dfee16b5` (23 Oct 2026). https://github.com/jslint-org/jslint
- **What it says (2011):** the message table has `used_before_a: "'{a}' was used before it was defined."`. The docs page (section "var"): "JSLint expects that a var will be declared" "only once, and that it will be declared before it is used." and "JSLint expects that a function will be declared before it is used." Under "Scope", because JavaScript then had only function scope, "it is wiser to declare all of a function's variables" "at the top of the function." There was no option to exempt functions.
- **What it says (2026):** the same text is now the `temporal_dead_zone_a` warning. A comment in `name_lookup`, dated 29 Sep 2026, records a deliberate deviation from the spec: a function reading a `let` declared below it warns, "though valid when called later." The comment says this matches ESLint's `no-use-before-define`. A function name is marked alive only when its declaration is reached, so a call above the declaration warns.
- **Caveat:** JSLint's 2011 advice is about `var` in a language without block scope; the reasoning ("blocks do not introduce a scope") no longer holds for `let`/`const`.
- **For the post:** ESLint's message is JSLint's sentence, word for word. JSLint's own maintainers now describe the deferred-reference case as valid code the rule reports anyway.
- **Text:** `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/src/jslint/jslint-6610addf.js`, `lint-6610addf.txt`, `jslint-dfee16b5.mjs`

### 3.2 JSHint's `latedef` (W003) and its `"nofunc"` setting (Mar 2013). VERIFIED (issue, source at tag `2.13.6`)

- **Citation:** JSHint `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/src/options.js`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/src/scope-manager.js`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/src/messages.js` at tag `2.13.6`; `johnkpaul`, "Add support for exclusion of function declarations from `latedef`," jshint/jshint #941, 20–25 Mar 2013. https://github.com/jshint/jshint/issues/941
- **What it says:** the option doc: "This option prohibits the use of a variable before it was defined." Because all variables are hoisted to the top of the function, "This behavior can lead to some very nasty bugs". "Setting this option to 'nofunc' will allow function declarations to be ignored." W003 reads "'{a}' was used before it was defined." The code warns for functions only when `latedef === true`; a use only inside a nested function is treated as "not necessarily an error, just latedef".
- **Why `nofunc` exists (#941):** "I often write function declarations at the bottom of my IIFE" so "that are hoisted throughout the current function scope." Another commenter (`guyzmo`) thought "it is a good habit to use symbols _after_ they are being defined," "But that's more a stylistic issue,". Anton Kovalyov (`valueof`) landed it: "To ignore function declarations you'll be able to use" `latedef:"nofunc"`.
- **For the post:** by 2013 the JSHint users already split the question: using a `var` before its line is a bug risk; calling a hoisted function declaration before its line is a style choice.
- **Text:** `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/src/jshint/options-2.13.6.js`, `scope-manager-2.13.6.js`, `messages-2.13.6.js`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/text/gh/jshint_jshint-941.txt`

### 3.3 The hoisting article both JSHint and ESLint cite recommends one `var` per scope, not a function style. ANECDOTAL (blog post, 2010)

- **Citation:** Ben Cherry, "JavaScript Scoping and Hoisting," adequatelygood.com, 8 Feb 2010. https://www.adequatelygood.com/JavaScript-Scoping-and-Hoisting.html (linked from JSHint's `latedef` doc and as `further_reading` in ESLint's `func-style` docs at `v10.13.0`).
- **What it says:** it shows that a `var` function expression called early throws `TypeError "foo is not a function"` while a declaration "will run!", because only the declaration's body is hoisted. Its advice: "I strongly recommend that you have exactly one var statement per scope," "and that it be at the top." and use JSLint's `onevar` option.
- **Caveat:** the article predates `let`/`const`; its advice is about `var` placement. It does not recommend function expressions over declarations.
- **For the post:** ESLint's only further reading for `func-style` explains hoisting but does not argue for either style.
- **Text:** `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/text/web/cherry-hoisting.txt`

### 3.4 ESLint's `no-use-before-define` (Aug 2013) and how each option was added. VERIFIED (source and docs at `v10.13.0`, commits, issues, changelog)

- **Citation:** Ilya Volodin, `32b13edc` "Rule No use before define" (24 Aug 2013), released in ESLint v0.0.9 (5 Oct 2013). Docs `docs/src/rules/no-use-before-define.md` and source `lib/rules/no-use-before-define.js` at `v10.13.0`. Options: `"nofunc"` (#829, `b1551d4e`, v0.6.0, 17 May 2014); object form with `functions` and `classes` (#3944, `12df7a7b`, v2.0.0-alpha-1, 11 Dec 2015); `variables` (#7111, PR #7948, v3.15.0, 3 Feb 2017); `allowNamedExports` (#15710, PR #15953, v8.17.0, 3 Jun 2022); TypeScript options `enums`, `typedefs`, `ignoreTypeReferences` (PR #19566, v9.28.0, 30 May 2025); IIFE bodies treated as the enclosing execution context (PR #21238, v10.13.0, 16 Oct 2026). https://eslint.org/docs/latest/rules/no-use-before-define
- **The docs' rationale:** before ES6, hoisting meant identifiers could be used before their declarations; "This can be confusing and some believe it is best" "to always declare variables and functions before using them." For `let`/`const` the docs mention the temporal dead zone and `ReferenceError`. The message is JSLint's: `'{{name}}' was used before it was defined.` Type `problem`; not frozen; not in `recommended` (`rules_meta.json`).
- **Why `functions: false` exists:** the request (#829, Conrad Zimmerman, Apr 2014) asked to match JSHint's `latedef: "nofunc"`: "This pattern is commonly used in asynchronous code," linking callbackhell.com. The docs now say: "Function declarations are hoisted, so it's safe to disable this option" and that some idiomatic patterns, such as mutual recursion, "are incompatible with enabling this option".
- **Why `classes` and `variables` exist:** both skip references from an inner function scope (a deferred reference) while still reporting same-scope uses. Toru Nagashima designed the object form in #3944 after noting `let a = new A(); class A {}` is a runtime error. Feross Aboukhadijeh asked for `variables` for standard: "There are just too many false positives for us" "when the latter example is disallowed." (the latter being a function body that reads a `var` declared below it). Teddy Katz (`not-an-aardvark`), championing it: it lets users avoid TDZ references without having "to adhere to the stylistic requirements of" the rule.
- **Defaults:** every check is on (`functions`, `classes`, `variables`, `enums`, `typedefs` true; `allowNamedExports` false; `ignoreTypeReferences` true). typescript-eslint 8.72.0's `no-use-before-define` has the same defaults (`scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/src/typescript-eslint/`).
- **For the post:** with the defaults, a `const` arrow that calls another `const` declared below it is reported even when nothing runs until both exist. `variables: false` is the option that separates code that will crash from code that only reads out of order, and it has existed since 2017.
- **Text:** `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/src/eslint/docs-no-use-before-define.md`, `src-no-use-before-define.js`, `rules_meta.json`, `rule_versions.json`, `CHANGELOG-v10.13.0.md`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/text/gh/commits-eslint-no-use-before-define.tsv`, `eslint_eslint-{829,3944,7111,15953,19566,21238}.txt`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/src/typescript-eslint/no-use-before-define-8.72.0.js`

### 3.5 ESLint maintainers on `no-use-before-define`: "intentionally designed to be a little more opinionated". VERIFIED (issues); ANECDOTAL as evidence of how common the complaints are

- **Method:** `gh api search/issues` for `no-use-before-define` in eslint/eslint titles (96 results, sorted by comments; `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/text/gh/search-eslint-nubd.txt`); the requests to relax the rule were opened and saved.
- **Mutual recursion (#1015, Jun 2014):** Michael Ficarra: "Mutually recursive functions are not a bad / problematic / hazardous pattern." Nicholas Zakas: "IMHO, part of the goal of the rule is to prevent things like this." Closed in 2015 because "Our current behavior matches JSHint's".
- **Mutual recursion again (#12473, Oct 2019):** Kevin Partington (`platinumazure`): "This rule is intentionally designed to be a little more opinionated" "than most of our rules." "It ignores hoisting and ensures that all of your references to a variable" "occur strictly after the declaration." The reporter: "Functions calling each other is pretty fundamental." The suggested rewrite was `var isOdd, isEven;` followed by assignments. Closed.
- **The stepdown rule (#10589, Jul 2018):** a request to let `const` arrows be read top-down, citing the "stepdown rule" from Robert C. Martin's _Clean Code_; the reporter notes declarations get around it. Jordan Harband (`ljharb`): "there's no such thing as an arrow function declaration." Auto-closed for lack of interest.
- **Top-level variables (#19968, Jul 2025, not planned):** Zakas: the larger issue is that "arrow functions are lazily evaluated" "so any variable declared later in source order would be flagged."
- **A false negative (#20014, Aug 2025, open, labelled `accepted`):** a callback passed to a function that runs it during the initializer reads the variable being initialized: "No lint error. Will error at runtime." A PR adding `allowDeferredReferences` (#20487, Feb 2026) was closed pending a design.
- **For the post:** the maintainers describe the rule as an ordering rule that ignores hoisting by design. It reports safe out-of-order references and can miss a real TDZ crash through a callback; Zakas's own summary is that it flags any later-declared variable read from an arrow body.
- **Text:** `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/text/gh/eslint_eslint-{1015,12473,10589,19968,20014,20487,13261}.txt`

### 3.6 The JSLint-to-ESLint chain for `no-use-before-define`. Summary

| Year | Tool | What changed | Why (source) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| by 2011 | JSLint | "'{a}' was used before it was defined." for vars and functions | one function scope; declare at the top (3.1) |
| Mar 2013 | JSHint | `latedef: "nofunc"` | functions placed at the bottom on purpose (3.2) |
| Aug 2013 | ESLint | rule added, JSLint's message | port of JSHint/JSLint (3.4) |
| May 2014 | ESLint | `"nofunc"` | match JSHint; callback-heavy async code (3.4) |
| Dec 2015 | ESLint | `functions`, `classes` | classes have a TDZ; outer-scope references are safe (3.4) |
| Feb 2017 | ESLint | `variables` | standard: too many false positives (3.4) |
| Jun 2022 | ESLint | `allowNamedExports` | `export { x }` above `const x` is safe (3.4) |
| May 2025 | ESLint | TypeScript options | TypeScript support in core (3.4) |
| Oct 2026 | ESLint | IIFE bodies are not deferred | IIFEs run where written (3.4) |

### 3.7 `func-style` (ESLint v0.2.0, 1 Jan 2014) began as two maintainers' opposite preferences. VERIFIED (issue, commit, docs at `v10.13.0`)

- **Citation:** Ilya Volodin, "Rule: function-style," eslint/eslint #460, 17 Dec 2013; Nicholas C. Zakas, `28eb460d` "Rule to enforce function style (fixes #460)," 30 Dec 2013; ESLint v0.2.0, 1 Jan 2014. Docs `docs/src/rules/func-style.md` and source `lib/rules/func-style.js` at `v10.13.0`. https://github.com/eslint/eslint/issues/460
- **The origin:** Volodin: the rule should be "configurable and should be off by default." Their reason: Zakas "prefers FunctionDeclaration, and my personal preference is FunctionExpression," "I keep forgetting to change them to FunctionDeclarations before submitting pull requests." Zakas scoped it to "a loose function that exists within some scope and how it should be written."
- **The default:** `"expression"` is the code default from the first commit; in Dec 2015 `eslint:recommended` still listed it as `[0, "declaration"]` (off) (#4834, #4874). No reason for either default is recorded.
- **The docs' rationale (unchanged in substance since 2014):** the difference is that declarations are hoisted; "There is really no correct or incorrect choice here, it is just a preference." The only further reading is Ben Cherry's article (3.3). Its "When Not To Use It" section: disable it to "allow developers to each decide how they want to write functions".
- **For the post:** the rule exists to enforce whichever preference a team has. Its inventors held opposite preferences, and the docs decline to pick one.
- **Text:** `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/text/gh/eslint_eslint-460.txt`, `eslint_eslint-4834.txt`, `eslint_eslint-4874.txt`, `commits-eslint-func-style.tsv`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/src/eslint/docs-func-style.md`, `src-func-style.js`, `CHANGELOG-v10.13.0.md`

### 3.8 `func-style`'s options and exceptions, and the request behind each. VERIFIED (issues, PRs, changelog, source at `v10.13.0`)

- **Arrows that use `this` (#3819, v1.5.1, 22 Sep 2015):** Toru Nagashima reported that `"declaration"` flagged arrows using lexical `this`, which a declaration cannot replace. Arrows containing `this` or `super` are now skipped in `"declaration"` mode (`super` added in PR #18473, v9.4.0, 31 May 2024).
- **`allowArrowFunctions` (#1897, v1.7.0, 16 Oct 2015):** `matthewwithanm` asked to allow single-expression arrows under `"declaration"`, "used primarily for exempting map/filter/reduce predicates." Zakas: "At first glance, this seems like a far too specific exception"; the compromise was exempting all arrows. It only applies with `"declaration"`.
- **`export default` exempt (#5183, v2.1.0, 15 Feb 2016):** the reporter argued the rule's benefit is "disallowing functions to be hoisted". Zakas disagreed: "This rule is just about style, and the docs explain the consequences" "not as a rationalization for the rule but for the edification of the reader." On hoisting, another rule "is a better indicator of this intent." All default-exported functions are skipped.
- **`overrides.namedExports` (#12829, Jordan Harband, Jan 2020; PR #18444, Percy Ma, v9.3.0, 17 May 2024):** Harband: "i don’t want equals signs in my export statements," "but i do want to prohibit declarations everywhere else." Values `"expression"`, `"declaration"`, `"ignore"`; the `overrides` shape was Milos Djermanovic's.
- **`allowTypeAnnotation` (#19719, `remcohaszing`, May 2025; PR #19754, v9.28.0, 30 May 2025):** lets `"declaration"` accept `const f: Fn = () => {}`, since a declaration cannot carry a whole-function type. The rule was frozen; Zakas was in favour of overriding "the frozen status of this rule for this particular purpose." `fasttime`: the annotated `const` "is the idiomatic and most concise way to ensure that a function" matches a signature.
- **Overloads (#19738, Josh Goldberg; PR #19755, v9.28.0):** `"expression"` no longer reports overloaded declarations; as `remcohaszing` put it, "always allow overloaded function declarations." "Otherwise it’s simply a TypeScript error."
- **No fixer, ever.** The rule has never had `fixable` (`commits-eslint-func-style.tsv`, source at `v10.13.0`); see 3.10 for why.
- **For the post:** every exception was added because the enforced style could not express something (lexical `this`, default exports, overloads, a function type). None was added because one style prevented bugs.
- **Text:** `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/text/gh/eslint_eslint-{3819,1897,5183,12829,18444,18473,19719,19754,19738,19755}.txt`

### 3.9 In `"expression"` mode, `allowArrowFunctions: true` does nothing, and ESLint plans to reject it in v11. VERIFIED (source and docs at `v10.13.0`, issues); my check (local run)

- **Citation:** `lib/rules/func-style.js` and docs at `v10.13.0`; `ehmicky`, eslint/eslint #13141 (Apr 2020); `HunterLarco`, PR #20687 (28 Mar 2026, closed); Tanuj Kanti (`Tanujkanti4441`), "Rule Change: make the `func-style` rule schema strict," #20689 (28 Mar 2026, open).
- **What the code does:** arrow handlers are only installed when `allowArrowFunctions` is false, and they report only when the style is `"declaration"` or `overrides.namedExports` is `"declaration"`. The docs say the option "applies only when the string option is set to" `"declaration"`. In `"expression"` mode the rule reports `FunctionDeclaration`s except those directly under `export default` and overloads; generators are reported too, though `const gen = function* () {}` is the only expression form (arrows cannot be generators).
- **The 2026 issue:** #20689 uses exactly `["error", "expression", { "allowArrowFunctions": true }]` as its example and proposes that the rule "will not accept the config that has no effect on the linting." Djermanovic: "since this kind of changes are considered breaking per our policies," "I'm adding this to v11 board". It was prompted by #20687, whose author used `"expression"` because "our team exclusively uses arrow functions" and wanted generators and `never`-returning functions exempt, constructs that "simply can't be written as arrow functions." The reply: the rule "is feature frozen so it cannot accept new features."
- **Earlier:** #13141 asked for the opposite (make `allowArrowFunctions: false` forbid arrows under `"expression"`). Kai Cataldo: "this would effectively disallow variable declarations with arrow function expressions," "and I think that's beyond the scope of the rule."
- **My check (local run):** ESLint 10.13.0 with `["error","expression",{allowArrowFunctions:true}]` reported `function declared()`, `function* gen()`, `export function exported()` and the TypeScript `function failDecl(): never`, and did not report `export default function main()` or the overloaded `parse` (`scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/check/out-eslint.txt`). Removing `allowArrowFunctions` changes nothing in this mode, per the source.
- **For the post:** the setting many configs copy (including Ultracite's) carries an option that has no effect, and the "expression" style it enforces still has holes where JavaScript or TypeScript require a declaration.
- **Text:** `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/src/eslint/src-func-style.js`, `docs-func-style.md`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/text/gh/eslint_eslint-{13141,20687,20689}.txt`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/check/out-eslint.txt`

### 3.10 `func-style` is frozen and has no fixer because a conversion can change behaviour. VERIFIED (issues, policy posts, rule metadata at `v10.13.0`); my check (local run)

- **Citation:** Nicholas C. Zakas, "Changes to rules policies," ESLint blog, 22 May 2020 (`eslint/eslint.org` at `2fffe78c`); `docs/src/contribute/core-rules.md` "Frozen Rules" at `v10.13.0`; OliverJAsh, #15392 (Dec 2021); Cameron Steffen (`camsteffen`), "Change Request: Add autofix for func-style," #18478 (May 2024, not planned).
- **The policy:** "**Stylistic rules are frozen** - we won't be adding any more options" "to stylistic rules." "We've learned that there's no way to satisfy everyone's personal preferences," The 2026 docs: "When rules are feature complete, they are marked as frozen", meaning it "catches 80% or more of expected violations and covers the majority of common exceptions"; new options only to fix a bug or support new syntax. `func-style` is `frozen: true`; so are `func-name-matching`, `prefer-arrow-callback` and `arrow-body-style`; `no-use-before-define`, `func-names`, `no-inner-declarations` and `no-func-assign` are not. 53 rules are frozen in `rules_meta.json`.
- **Suggestions refused (#15392):** Zakas: "we actually aren't making any further updates to rules that enforce stylistic preferences."
- **Autofix refused (#18478):** `fasttime`: the rule is not autofixable because "in some cases replacing a function declaration with a function expression" "or vice-versa is not guaranteed to preserve the original semantics." Their example: an arrow has no `prototype`, so `if (x.prototype)` changes when an arrow becomes a declaration; built-in fixes are withheld because "those _fixes_ have a potential to introduce hard-to-find bugs." On which style is usual: "My personal impression is that function declarations are more common." Steffen: "So in a way func-style feels like an artifact of a pre-ES6 world."
- **Other refused options:** `let`-arrows (#16159, 2022; #18570, 2024), arrows inside functions (#18046, 2024), anonymous functions (#17128, 2023), an `"arrow"` style (#3113, 2015; #6330, 2016, closed for lack of consensus), `allowArrowFunctions` as a string (#7765, 2016).
- **My check (local run):** Node 24.14.1: an arrow's `prototype` is `undefined`, a function expression's is an object, and `new` on an arrow throws `TypeError: arrowFn is not a constructor` (`scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/check/out-node-semantics.txt`).
- **For the post:** neither direction of the conversion is automatic in ESLint. Going from a declaration to a `const` arrow also moves the function into the temporal dead zone, which is the case 3.24's plugin guards against.
- **Text:** `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/src/eslint/blog-2020-05-22-changes-to-rules-policies.md`, `contribute-core-rules-v10.13.0.md`, `rules_meta.json`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/text/gh/eslint_eslint-{15392,18478,16159,18570,18046,17128,3113,6330,7765}.txt`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/check/out-node-semantics.txt`

### 3.11 `func-names` (ESLint v0.4.0, Feb 2014): named expressions for stack traces; `func-name-matching` (v3.8.0, Oct 2016). VERIFIED (docs and source at `v10.13.0`, commits, changelog); my check (local run)

- **Citation:** Kyle Nunery, `2058f251` "#595 add func-names rule" (8 Feb 2014), v0.4.0 (12 Feb 2014); `never` (#6059, v2.13.0), recursive names allowed (#6616, v3.1.1), inferred names recognised by `as-needed` (Logan Smyth, #7235, v3.10.0, 11 Nov 2016), `generators` (#9511, v5.6.0, 14 Sep 2018). `func-name-matching`: Annie Zhang, #6065, v3.8.0 (14 Oct 2016).
- **What `func-names` says:** "A pattern that's becoming more common" "is to give function expressions names to aid in debugging." "If you leave off the function name then when the function throws an exception" you are likely to see an anonymous function in the stack trace. Options: `"always"` (default), `"as-needed"` (only where the spec does not infer a name), `"never"`, plus `generators`. `"always"` and `"as-needed"` also require a name on `export default function`. Further reading includes a 2015 2ality post on ES6 function names. Not frozen.
- **`func-name-matching`:** requires `const foo = function foo() {}`-style names to match the variable or property. Frozen.
- **My check (local run):** Node 24.14.1 infers `name` for anonymous functions assigned to `const` (`["inferredArrow","inferredExpr"]`) and to an object property (`"handler"`), and gives an inline callback the empty name `""` (`scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/check/out-node-semantics.txt`).
- **For the post:** since ES2015, assigning to a `const` names the function, so the stack-trace argument for `"always"` is weaker than when the rule was written; `"as-needed"` encodes that. With `"always"`, `func-style: "expression"` forces `const f = function f() {}` or an arrow; `func-names` does not apply to arrows.
- **Text:** `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/src/eslint/docs-func-names.md`, `src-func-names.js`, `docs-func-name-matching.md`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/text/gh/commits-eslint-func-names.tsv`, `commits-eslint-func-name-matching.tsv`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/check/out-node-semantics.txt`

### 3.12 `prefer-arrow-callback` (v1.2.0, Aug 2015) and `arrow-body-style` (v1.8.0, Oct 2015): the two function rules with fixers. VERIFIED (docs and source at `v10.13.0`, commits, changelog)

- **Citation:** Toru Nagashima, `fca96e5e` "New: `prefer-arrow-callback` rule (fixes #3140)," v1.2.0 (18 Aug 2015); `allowNamedFunctions` (#5675, v2.8.0, 15 Apr 2016); `allowUnboundThis` (#4668, v2.9.0, 29 Apr 2016); fixer (#7002, Teddy Katz, v3.5.0, 9 Sep 2016). alberto, `c97c4c0f` "New: arrow-body-style rule (fixes #4109)," v1.8.0 (30 Oct 2015); `never` (#6317) and `requireReturnForObjectLiteral` (#5936), both v2.12.0 (10 Jun 2016); fixer (#7240, v3.9.0, 28 Oct 2016). Both rules are frozen.
- **`prefer-arrow-callback`:** arrows are "less verbose, and easier to reason about." It only looks at callbacks: "Instances where an arrow function would not produce identical results will be ignored." It skips generators, named functions that call themselves, functions that use `arguments`, `super` or `new.target`, and (by default, `allowUnboundThis: true`) functions that use `this` without `.bind(this)`. The fixer also refuses when `this` is used without `.bind(this)` ("so don't perform any fixes."), when parameters are duplicated (sloppy mode), and when the first parameter is a TypeScript `this` parameter. Default `{ allowNamedFunctions: false, allowUnboundThis: true }`.
- **`arrow-body-style`:** `"as-needed"` (default) removes braces around a lone `return`; `"always"` and `"never"` force one form; `requireReturnForObjectLiteral` (with `"as-needed"`) keeps braces for returned object literals. Its fixer rewrites the body.
- **For the post:** the rule that would turn a declaration into an arrow (`func-style`) has no fixer, while the rules that tidy arrows once they exist do. Concise bodies (dropping the curly braces) are only available to arrows, which is maintainer claim (1).
- **Text:** `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/src/eslint/docs-prefer-arrow-callback.md`, `src-prefer-arrow-callback.js`, `docs-arrow-body-style.md`, `src-arrow-body-style.js`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/text/gh/commits-eslint-prefer-arrow-callback.tsv`, `commits-eslint-arrow-body-style.tsv`

### 3.13 `no-inner-declarations` in ESLint 9: block-level functions allowed in strict code, rule dropped from `recommended`. VERIFIED (issue, PRs, docs at `v10.13.0`, migration guide)

- **Citation:** Brandon Mills, `694c8175` "Rule: no-inner-declarations (fixes #587)," v0.6.0 (17 May 2014); `Trott`, #15576 (Feb 2022); Tanuj Kanti, PR #17885 (new default and option) and Milos Djermanovic, PR #17920 (removal from `eslint:recommended`), both in v9.0.0 (5 Apr 2024).
- **What changed:** `Trott` pointed out that block-level functions are widely supported and "That part of the rule is the only part that's enabled by default." Djermanovic agreed that "disallowing block-scoped function declarations looks like just a stylistic preference," except in sloppy scripts, where Annex B semantics make a block function behave like a `var`. ESLint 9 added `blockScopedFunctions` (default `"allow"`: allowed in strict code with ES2015+) and removed the rule from `recommended`.
- **Docs slip:** the v9 migration guide calls the option `blockScopeFunctions`; the rule, its docs and the codemod use `blockScopedFunctions`.
- **For the post:** in ES modules, `no-inner-declarations` now reports only `var` in blocks if set to `"both"`, and nothing with its defaults, so it adds little next to `func-style: "expression"`, which already reports every non-default-export declaration.
- **Text:** `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/text/gh/eslint_eslint-{15576,17885,17920}.txt`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/src/eslint/docs-no-inner-declarations.md`, `src-no-inner-declarations.js`, `migrate-to-9.0.0.md`

### 3.14 `no-func-assign`: guards a declaration that a `const` makes unassignable anyway. VERIFIED (docs at `v10.13.0`)

- **Citation:** `docs/src/rules/no-func-assign.md` at `v10.13.0` (in v0.0.9, 2013; in `eslint:recommended`; `handled_by_typescript: true`). Oxlint `no_func_assign.rs` (`correctness`, recommended); Biome `noFunctionAssign` (`suspicious`).
- **What it says:** reassigning a `function` declaration "is often indicative of a mistake or issue." It reports `function foo() {}; foo = bar;` and assignments to a named function expression's own name, but `let foo = function () {}; foo = bar;` is correct code for the rule.
- **For the post:** under `func-style: "expression"` with `const` (and `prefer-const`), a function binding cannot be reassigned at all: the language throws, so this rule is mostly redundant there. It is one of the few places where the expression style plus `const` gives a guarantee a declaration does not.
- **Text:** `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/src/eslint/docs-no-func-assign.md`, `src-no-func-assign.js`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/src/oxc/eslint_no_func_assign.rs`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/src/biome/no_function_assign.rs`

### 3.15 Oxlint's `func-style` (Feb 2025) matches ESLint on the three edge cases, and has no fix. VERIFIED (source at `oxlint_v1.89.0`, commit list); my check (local run); Vendor (VoidZero/Oxc)

- **Citation:** yefan, "feat(linter): implement func-style rule," oxc-project/oxc #8977 (12 Feb 2025; declared `version = "0.15.11"`); `crates/oxc_linter/src/rules/eslint/func_style.rs` at `oxlint_v1.89.0`.
- **What it does:** category `style`, `fix = pending`. Options mirror ESLint's: style (default `expression`), `allowArrowFunctions`, `allowTypeAnnotation`, `overrides.namedExports`. On the three points: (1) arrows reach the reporting code only for `"declaration"` or `namedExports: "declaration"`, so `allowArrowFunctions` is a no-op in `"expression"` mode (its doc comment says arrows are allowed "regardless of the style setting"); (2) a declaration whose parent is `ExportDefaultDeclaration` is never reported; (3) overload signatures (no body) are skipped, and so is any declaration whose symbol has redeclarations, which covers the overloaded implementation. The help text repeats the rule description.
- **My check (local run):** Oxlint 1.89.0 reported exactly the same `func-style` lines as ESLint on my files (`scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/check/out-oxlint.txt`).
- **For the post:** Oxlint copied ESLint's behaviour, including the no-op option.
- **Text:** `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/src/oxc/eslint_func_style.rs`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/text/gh/commits-oxc-eslint_func_style.tsv`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/check/out-oxlint.txt`

### 3.16 Oxlint's `no-use-before-define` (Feb 2026) ran only on TypeScript files until Oxlint 1.81.0 (Sep 2026); the other ports. VERIFIED (source at `oxlint_v1.89.0`, PRs, tag comparisons); Vendor (VoidZero/Oxc)

- **Citation:** `camc314`: "feat(linter): implement typescript/no-use-before-define," #19373 (merged 13 Feb 2026, first in `oxlint_v1.48.0`); "fix(linter): move no-use-before-define to eslint plugin," #19438 (merged 19 Feb 2026, `oxlint_v1.49.0`); "fix(linter/eslint/no-use-before-define): run on JS, JSX files," #26114 (merged 26 Aug 2026, fixing #26113; in `oxlint_v1.81.0`, not `1.80.0`). Release membership from `gh api compare` (`scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/text/gh/oxc-no-use-before-define-release-check.txt`).
- **What #26114 says:** after the move, "its TypeScript-only execution gate was retained." As a result, "eslint/no-use-before-define never ran for JavaScript or JSX files."
- **The rule at 1.89.0:** category `restriction`; options and defaults identical to ESLint's, including the TypeScript ones and `"nofunc"` (string form fixed in #23115, Jun 2026). Rationale: "Referencing identifiers before their declarations can hide bugs and" "make code order-dependent and difficult to reason about." Help: "Move the declaration before any references to it," "or remove the reference if it is not needed." It adds "used here" and "defined here" labels; ESLint gives only the message.
- **The other ports (first commits, declared version, category, fix):** `func-names` #4618 (4 Aug 2024; `style`; conditional suggestion); `arrow-body-style` #11937 (28 Jun 2025; `style`; fix); `func-name-matching` #21708 (25 Apr 2026; 1.62.0; `style`; none); `prefer-arrow-callback` #22312 (13 May 2026; 1.65.0; `style`; fix; help "Use an arrow function instead."); `no-inner-declarations` (Jul 2023; `pedantic`; has `blockScopedFunctions`); `unicorn/consistent-function-scoping` #4948 (19 Aug 2024; `suspicious`; `checkArrowFunctions` default true; ignores functions with JSX); `react/function-component-definition` #24471 (14 Jul 2026; 1.75.0; `style`; conditional suggestion only; defaults `function-declaration` / `function-expression`).
- **For the post:** a preset that set `no-use-before-define` in Oxlint between Feb and Sep 2026 got it only on `.ts`/`.tsx` files. Its help text tells the reader (or an agent) to move the declaration up, the same move for a crash and for a safe forward reference.
- **Text:** `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/text/gh/oxc-project_oxc-{19373,19438,26114,26113,23115}.txt`, `oxc-no-use-before-define-release-check.txt`, `commits-oxc-*.tsv`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/src/oxc/*.rs`

### 3.17 Biome's `useArrowFunction` (1.0.0): converts expressions, never declarations, with a "safe" fix that users keep breaking. VERIFIED (source at `@biomejs/biome@2.6.3`, issues, PRs)

- **Citation:** `crates/biome_js_analyze/src/lint/complexity/use_arrow_function.rs` at `c5c3383d` (version 1.0.0, `recommended: true`, severity warning, `fix_kind: FixKind::Safe`, source `prefer-arrow-callback` `inspired()`); issues #7616 (Sep 2025, `arguments`), #9585 (Mar 2026, open, constructor via `new`), #12372 (Oct 2026, open, `.prototype`); PRs #8009 (merged 6 Nov 2025), #9652 and #12383 (both closed unmerged).
- **What it converts:** "This rule proposes turning all function expressions that are not generators" (`function*`) and that don't use `this` into arrows. "Named function expressions are ignored", and so are functions that use `arguments` (which arrows lack) and functions with a TypeScript `this` parameter. "This rule does not modify top-level function declarations", linking discussion #7108.
- **Fix safety:** #8009 added checks for `arguments`, `this` and `new.target`. #9585's reproduction: after the fix, `new Foo()` throws "TypeError: Foo is not a constructor". #12372 cites Biome's own promise that "Safe fixes are guaranteed to not change the semantic of your code." Two PRs to mark the fix unsafe were closed without merging; at 2.6.3 it is still `Safe`.
- **My check (local run):** Biome 2.6.3 offered a "Safe fix" turning `const expr = function () { return 3; }` into `const expr = () => 3;` (`scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/check/out-biome.txt`).
- **For the post:** the arrow conversion is semantics-preserving only if nothing constructs the function or reads its `prototype`, which a lint rule cannot always see; ESLint's equivalent fixer is limited to callbacks.
- **Text:** `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/src/biome/use_arrow_function.rs`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/text/gh/biomejs_biome-{7616,8009,9585,9652,12372,12383}.txt`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/check/out-biome.txt`

### 3.18 Biome's `noInvalidUseBeforeDeclaration` (1.5.0) reports only uses that would crash, yet claims to be the "same" as `no-use-before-define`. VERIFIED (source at `@biomejs/biome@2.6.3`, PR); my check (local run)

- **Citation:** `Conaclos`, "feat(lint/noInvalidUseBeforeDeclaration): add rule," biomejs/biome #1258 (merged 20 Dec 2023); `crates/biome_js_analyze/src/lint/correctness/no_invalid_use_before_declaration.rs` at `c5c3383d`; Biome's ESLint migration table `crates/biome_cli/src/execute/migrate/eslint_any_rule_to_biome.rs`.
- **What it reports:** block-scoped variables, parameters, classes and enums used before their declaration, plus `var` read before its declaration ("This is likely an error"). The PR states its limit: "It cannot report cases where a function that uses a variable" "is called before the declaration of the variable." Its docs list as valid `f(); function f() {}`, `export { CONSTANT }` above the `const`, and a function body that reads a later `const`. No options. Recommended, severity error.
- **The mapping:** its sources are `RuleSource::Eslint("no-use-before-define").same()` and the typescript-eslint rule `.same()`, and `biome migrate eslint` maps `no-use-before-define` to it. With ESLint's defaults, the two rules disagree on every one of those valid examples. The PR says the rule was requested in a comment on discussion #3 (Biome's thread mapping rules from other linters); I could not load that comment, only the discussion's mapping table.
- **My check (local run):** on my `a.js`, ESLint and Oxlint reported 5 use-before-define references (including mutual recursion and a `const` read inside a function body); Biome 2.6.3 reported 1, the top-level `tooEarly()` call, which is the only one that throws at runtime. Its help: "Using a variable before it is declared makes the code depend on declaration order" "and hoisting behavior." (`scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/check/out-biome.txt`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/check/out-eslint.txt`).
- **For the post:** Biome built the TDZ check, not the ordering rule. A Biome user migrating an ESLint config with `no-use-before-define` silently gets the weaker check.
- **Text:** `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/text/gh/biomejs_biome-1258.txt`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/src/biome/no_invalid_use_before_declaration.rs`, `eslint_any_rule_to_biome.rs`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/check/out-biome.txt`

### 3.19 Biome's `func-style`: refused in 2023, written by an AI model in 2026, still in nursery. VERIFIED (PR, issues, discussions as saved HTML, source at `@biomejs/biome@2.6.3`)

- **Citation:** `SorenHolstHansen`, "Implement use-function-convention," #975 (30 Nov 2023, closed as not planned with a pointer to the rule-proposal discussion); discussion #7108 "Extend useArrowFunction rule to cover top-level function declarations" (`schickling`, 5 Aug 2025, 31 votes); discussion #8963 "Function style: Prefer function, allow for arrow functions for closures" (Feb 2026); `dyc3`, "feat(lint/js): add useConsistentFunctionStyle," #11642 (merged 10 Sep 2026); `nursery/use_consistent_function_style.rs` (version 2.5.14) and `nursery/use_named_function.rs` (version 2.6.0) at `c5c3383d`.
- **Demand ran both ways:** #7108 wanted arrows everywhere (its example config is `func-style: ["error", "expression", { allowArrowFunctions: true }]`); #8963's team prefers `function` for non-callbacks and had written a GritQL plugin, though "we would rather not have to maintain this ourselves".
- **The port:** `dyc3`: "I intentionally didn't port all the options." The PR body includes an investigation "my clanker" did of ESLint's history, concluding "This is a historical default, not evidence of a technical recommendation." Its recommendation, which `dyc3` adopted: "automatically allow variables with type annotations, and defer named-export overrides until there’s demand." The PR says "implemented by gpt 6 astra".
- **The rule:** "A consistent function style makes function definitions easier to recognize." Options `style` (default `"expression"`) and `allowArrowFunctions`; no `namedExports`. "In both styles, callbacks, methods, and default exports are ignored." TypeScript "overloads are allowed because they require declarations." In `"declaration"` mode typed variables are allowed because a declaration "cannot be annotated with an existing function type." It has no fix. `useNamedFunction` (from `func-names`, `inspired()`) asks for a name even where JavaScript infers one.
- **My check (local run):** with the nursery rule at `style: "expression"`, Biome reported the same four declarations as ESLint and Oxlint (`scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/check/out-biome.txt`).
- **For the post:** the newest implementation of the rule was ported by an AI model from an AI-written history, which found no recorded reason for the default.
- **Text:** `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/text/gh/biomejs_biome-{975,7107,11642,12460}.txt`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/text/web/biome-discussion-7108.txt`, `biome-discussion-8963.txt`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/src/biome/biome_js_analyze_src_lint_nursery_use_consistent_function_style.rs`, `biome_js_analyze_src_lint_nursery_use_named_function.rs`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/check/out-biome.txt`

### 3.20 Biome's other function rules and its ESLint mapping. VERIFIED (source at `@biomejs/biome@2.6.3`, PRs)

- **`useConsistentArrowReturn`** (`style`, version 2.2.3, from issue #5050, a port request for `arrow-body-style`; options PR #7457 merged 23 Sep 2025): `style` `asNeeded` (default), `always`, `never`, and `requireForObjectLiteral` (only with `asNeeded`); skips bodies with directives or comments and bare `return;`; safe fix; not recommended; severity information.
- **`noFunctionAssign`** (`suspicious`) and **`noInnerDeclarations`** (`correctness`, 1.0.0, `same()` as ESLint): the latter allows block-level function declarations in ES modules.
- **ESLint mapping (`eslint_any_rule_to_biome.rs`):** `func-style` → nursery `useConsistentFunctionStyle` (inspired); `func-names` → nursery `useNamedFunction` (inspired); `prefer-arrow-callback` → `useArrowFunction` (inspired); `arrow-body-style` → `useConsistentArrowReturn`; `no-use-before-define` → `noInvalidUseBeforeDeclaration`; `no-func-assign` → `noFunctionAssign`; `no-inner-declarations` → `noInnerDeclarations`. In discussion #3's mapping table (saved 29 Oct 2026) `func-style` has no Biome rule listed.
- **For the post:** only the arrow-tidying rules are stable in Biome; the style rule and the naming rule are nursery.
- **Text:** `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/src/biome/use_consistent_arrow_return.rs`, `biome_rule_options_src_use_consistent_arrow_return.rs`, `no_function_assign.rs`, `no_inner_declarations.rs`, `eslint_any_rule_to_biome.rs`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/text/web/biome-discussion-3-comment.txt`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/text/gh/biomejs_biome-{5050,7457}.txt`

### 3.21 `react/function-component-definition` (eslint-plugin-react 7.18.0, Jan 2020): proposed to forbid arrow components. VERIFIED (issue, PR, docs and source at `v7.37.5`); my check (local run)

- **Citation:** `tleunen`, "Rule proposal: Warn against using an arrow function/anon fn when declaring a component," jsx-eslint/eslint-plugin-react #690 (18 Jul 2016); Stefan Wullems, PR #2414 (merged 11 Jan 2020 by Jordan Harband, `99cea84c`); changelog entry under 7.18.0 (15 Jan 2020). Docs and `lib/rules/function-component-definition.js` at `v7.37.5`.
- **Origin:** "Component should be named, and arrow functions are anonymous functions," "so they produce unnamed components." `gaearon`: "Per spec, the name _will_ be assigned, so it’s fine." and "the official position is that arrow functions work well as components,". Jordan Harband (`ljharb`): "In general, explicit > implicit," and in the Babel REPL `export default (props) => ...` had no name.
- **Options:** `namedComponents` (`"function-declaration"` default, `"function-expression"`, `"arrow-function"`, or an array) and `unnamedComponents` (`"function-expression"` default or `"arrow-function"`). "By default it prefers function declarations for named components" "and function expressions for unnamed components."
- **Fixer limits:** "There is one unfixable pattern in JavaScript.": `export default function Name()` cannot become an arrow in place. In TypeScript, typed variables are not fixed to declarations, and a component with one unconstrained type parameter is not fixed to an arrow (the `<T>` would read as JSX).
- **My check (local run):** with `namedComponents: "arrow-function"` and the default `unnamedComponents`, ESLint 10.13.0 and Oxlint 1.89.0 (with suggestions applied) rewrote `export default () => <div />` into `export default function() { return <div /> }`; the next run reported that same component as "not an arrow function" (unfixable) and `func-names` reported "Unexpected unnamed function." (`scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/check/out-eslint-fix-chain.txt`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/check/out-oxlint-fix-chain.txt`).
- **For the post:** the rule was proposed against arrows and defaults to declarations; set to arrows, its own fixer can produce code the same rule then rejects unless `unnamedComponents` is also set.
- **Text:** `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/text/gh/jsx-eslint_eslint-plugin-react-{690,2414}.txt`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/src/react/`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/check/out-eslint-fix-chain.txt`, `out-oxlint-fix-chain.txt`

### 3.22 `unicorn/consistent-function-scoping` (2019): hoist helpers out, which `no-use-before-define` then puts above their caller. VERIFIED (issue, PR, docs and source at `v79.0.0`); my check (local run)

- **Citation:** futpib, "Rule proposal: Consistent function scoping," sindresorhus/eslint-plugin-unicorn #230 (31 Jan 2019, IssueHunt bounty $80); Adam Babcock (`MrHen`), PR #309 (merged 5 Sep 2019). Docs `docs/rules/consistent-function-scoping.md` and `rules/consistent-function-scoping.js` at `v79.0.0`.
- **What it says:** "A function definition should be placed as close to the top-level scope as possible" "without breaking its captured values." It claims this "improves readability," and "directly improves performance", linking a 2008 Stack Overflow answer and a Pony Foo article on V8 (I did not open either). `checkArrowFunctions` (default `true`) was requested in #596 (Mar 2020; closed as completed in Dec 2020). It ignores IIFEs, React built-in hook callbacks, `jest.mock()`, `vi.mock()` and `vi.hoisted()` factories. In unicorn's `recommended`.
- **The pushback:** #596: "Sometimes I create a one liner function," "and I don't want to have to move it to the highest scope." Extracting it means "then it would be moved far away from the" function that uses it. fisker (#1074, 2021) found "there are too many cases 'in theory' can move out," "but people usually won't."; their proposal to check only declarations was closed as not planned in Jun 2026.
- **My check (local run):** both linters reported the inner arrow in `outer`; on the returned arrow component in `c.jsx`, ESLint's unicorn 79 reported it and Oxlint did not (Oxlint skips functions containing JSX) (`scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/check/out-eslint.txt`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/check/out-oxlint.txt`).
- **For the post:** with `const` arrows and `no-use-before-define`, the hoisted helper must go above its first caller, so this rule and the ordering rule together push helpers to the top of the file.
- **Text:** `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/text/gh/sindresorhus_eslint-plugin-unicorn-{230,309,596,1074}.txt`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/src/unicorn/`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/check/out-eslint.txt`, `out-oxlint.txt`

### 3.23 `antfu/top-level-function`: the opposite rule, also a stated preference. VERIFIED (source and docs at `v3.3.1`, issues, npm API); ANECDOTAL

- **Citation:** Anthony Fu, eslint-plugin-antfu `src/rules/top-level-function.{md,ts}` at tag `v3.3.1` (published 23 Oct 2026); antfu/eslint-config #178 (Apr 2023); antfu/eslint-plugin-antfu #35 and PR #37 (function expressions covered, Feb 2025). npm downloads for 22–28 Oct 2026: 891,241 (`api.npmjs.org`).
- **What it does:** reports a top-level `const` (or exported `const`) holding an arrow or function expression and autofixes it to a `function` declaration. Exemptions: a typed variable ("When the variable is assigned with types, it rule will ignore it." (sic)) and an expression-bodied arrow that fits on one line.
- **The reason:** asked why (#178), Fu: "It's my **opinionated** config." and later "For consistency." "I consider top-level arrow function is a bad practice." The asker's view was the maintainer's (3) in reverse: "using hoisting to break the function declare order is not a good habit." #35's reporter liked that the fix lets functions be "automatically promoted to the top to avoid undefined situations."
- **For the post:** the two most visible opinionated configs pull in opposite directions (Fu's toward declarations, Ultracite's toward `const` arrows), and both owners call it preference. The declaration direction can be autofixed safely, since it only adds hoisting.
- **Text:** `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/src/antfu/`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/text/gh/antfu_eslint-config-178.txt`, `antfu_eslint-plugin-antfu-35.txt`, `commits-antfu-tlf.tsv`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/src/prefer-arrow/downloads-last-week-eslint-plugin-antfu.json`

### 3.24 `eslint-plugin-prefer-arrow` and `eslint-plugin-prefer-arrow-functions`: the arrow-everywhere plugins, and the TDZ guard. VERIFIED (npm metadata and READMEs, source at tag `3.10.2`)

- **Citation:** `TristonJ`, eslint-plugin-prefer-arrow 1.2.3 (last published 26 Jan 2021); `JamieMason`, eslint-plugin-prefer-arrow-functions 3.10.2 (published 9 Aug 2026), `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/src/guard.ts` at tag `3.10.2`. Weekly downloads 22–28 Oct 2026 from `api.npmjs.org`: 1,124,896 and 511,831.
- **prefer-arrow:** reports `function` everywhere except prototype members (unless `disallowPrototype`) and functions that use `this`; `allowStandaloneDeclarations` exempts top-level declarations; it autofixes only with `singleReturnOnly`, which targets functions where conversion "would dramatically simplify the code."
- **prefer-arrow-functions:** "auto-fix plain Functions into Arrow Functions, in all cases where conversion would result" "in the same behaviour". In 3.10.2 it skips generators, overloads, `this`/`arguments`/`super`/`new.target` users, and declarations a `const` would break: "const removes hoisting: code above the declaration which eagerly uses the name" "would hit the TDZ".
- **For the post:** a tool that does convert declarations to `const` arrows has to check for eager use above the declaration; the core rules avoid the problem by not fixing at all.
- **Text:** `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/src/prefer-arrow/README-eslint-plugin-prefer-arrow-npm.md`, `README-eslint-plugin-prefer-arrow-functions-npm.md`, `pafn-3.10.2-guard.ts`, `npm-*.json`, `downloads-last-week-*.json`

### 3.25 TypeScript only narrows after a `never` call when the callee has an explicit type, so `const` arrows need a variable annotation. VERIFIED (PR, issue); my check (local run)

- **Citation:** `ahejlsberg`, "Assertions in control flow analysis," microsoft/TypeScript PR #32695 (merged 23 Sep 2019); Asaf-S, microsoft/TypeScript #55351 (Aug 2023, closed as duplicate), cited in ESLint PR #20687.
- **What it says:** a call is treated as an assertion or never-returning call only when "each identifier in the function name references an entity with an explicit type," "An entity is considered to have an explicit type" "when it is declared as a function, method, class or namespace," "or as a variable, parameter or property with an explicit type annotation." A commenter on #55351 (`MartinJohns`): "For performance reasons only identifiers with type annotations are analyzed."
- **My check (local run):** `tsc` 6.0.3 `--strict`: after `fail()` where `const fail = (m: string): never => {...}`, `return x` still errors (TS2322, `string | undefined`); after a `function failDecl(): never` declaration, and after `const failTyped: Fail = ...`, it narrows (`scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/check/out-tsc-never.txt`).
- **For the post:** for assertion and `never` helpers, the expression style needs a separate function type, which cuts against the claim that a function is easier to type through its variable; declarations get narrowing for free. Overloads (3.8) are the other construct only declarations can express.
- **Text:** `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/text/gh/microsoft_TypeScript-32695-body.txt`, `microsoft_TypeScript-55351.txt`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/check/ts/never.ts`, `out-tsc-never.txt`

### 3.26 The same files through ESLint, Oxlint and Biome with Ultracite-like options. My check (local run)

- **Method:** hand-written `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/check/src/a.js` (12 cases), `b.ts`, `c.jsx`, `d.jsx`; ESLint 10.13.0 (`scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/check/eslint.config.mjs`, `@typescript-eslint/no-use-before-define` on `.ts`), Oxlint 1.89.0 (`scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/check/oxlintrc.json`), Biome 2.6.3 (`scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/check/biome-ultracite/biome.json`, plus `biome-nursery/` for the nursery rules), all with `func-style: ["error","expression",{allowArrowFunctions:true}]`, `no-use-before-define: "error"`, `func-names`, `prefer-arrow-callback`, `arrow-body-style: "as-needed"`, `unicorn/consistent-function-scoping`, `react/function-component-definition` (`namedComponents: "arrow-function"`) or the Biome equivalents. Run 29 Oct 2026.

| Case | ESLint | Oxlint | Biome (stable) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `function declared()` | func-style | func-style | none (nursery rule reports) |
| `export default function main()` | not reported | not reported | not reported |
| `function* gen()` | func-style | func-style | none (nursery rule reports) |
| `const expr = function () {}` | func-names | func-names | useArrowFunction (safe fix) |
| `export default function main()` calls consts below | 2 use-before-define | 2 | none |
| `helper` calls `later` (declared below, safe) | use-before-define | use-before-define | none |
| `isEven`/`isOdd` mutual recursion | use-before-define | use-before-define | none |

| `function failDecl(): never` | func-style | func-style | none |
| `Shape` interface used before declaration | not reported | not reported | not reported |
| `Card` used in `Page` above it (`c.jsx`) | use-before-define | use-before-define | none |

- **What it shows:** ESLint and Oxlint agree line for line on `func-style`, `no-use-before-define`, `func-names`, `prefer-arrow-callback` and `arrow-body-style`; they differ only on `consistent-function-scoping` for a JSX-returning arrow (3.22). Of the 5 use-before-define reports in `a.js` (6 with `c.jsx`), 1 is a runtime crash; Biome reports only that one.
