### 9.2 What the 2022 combination meant, and still means: arrows everywhere a function doesn't need `this`. VERIFIED (config source; ESLint `--print-config`; my check)

- **The four rules work together.** `func-style` "expression" reports function declarations; `func-names` "always" reports unnamed function expressions, even when the name would be inferred (resolved options `[2, "always", {}]`); `func-name-matching` requires the name to match the variable; `prefer-arrow-callback` reports function expressions passed as callbacks. So the only non-arrow function a file can hold is a named function expression whose name repeats its variable (`const f = function f() {}`), a method, or one of `func-style`'s exemptions (9.6).
- **My check (29 Oct 2026, Oxlint 1.89.0 and ESLint 10.13.0 with Ultracite's core preset):** `export const increment = function () { this.count += 1; };` gets "Unexpected unnamed function." from both; a generator `const handler = function* gen() {}` gets "Function name `gen` should match variable name `handler`."; `[1, 2].map(function (n) { ... })` gets both `func-names` and "Unexpected function expression." (`prefer-arrow-callback`). Biome reports only the callback (`useArrowFunction`).
- **For the post:** the effective rule isn't _expressions_ but _arrows, unless the function needs `this`, `arguments`, a generator or a constructor_.
- **Text:** `scratchpad/func-style-lit/main/check/presets/oxlint-expr.txt`, `eslint-expr.txt`, `biome-expr.txt`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/main/check/eslint/resolved-core-a.ts.json`

### 9.3 #551: an options shape Oxlint didn't take, found when Oxlint started validating it (Jan–Feb 2026). VERIFIED (issue, PR, oxc PR and release notes)

- **What happened:** V7's Oxlint config had `"func-style": ["error", { "allowArrowFunctions": true, "style": "expression" }]`. Issue #551 (`Entropy-10`, 28 Jan 2026, Ultracite 7.1.1): `ultracite fix` "throws an error saying 'Failed to parse oxlint configuration file'", and "the 'func-style' option is misconfigured". An Oxlint maintainer (`connorshea`) explained: "this is due to the change to start enforcing config validation for oxlint rules", linking oxc PR #18390, "Update `eslint/func-style` to error on invalid configuration options." (merged 22 Jan 2026, shipped in Oxlint 1.42.0 on 26 Jan 2026). PR #552 (`PunGrumpy`) moved `"expression"` out of the object: "No behavior change beyond correcting the oxlint config schema."
- **Caveat:** the issue doesn't show what Oxlint did with the old shape before 1.42.0; "expression" is the default either way, so the effective style didn't change.
- **For the post:** nothing about function style. It shows the options were copied rather than considered: the `allowArrowFunctions` that survived the fix does nothing in "expression" mode (9.6).
- **Text:** `scratchpad/func-style-lit/main/src/gh/haydenbleasel_ultracite-551.json`, `-551-comments.json`, `-552.json`, `oxc-pr-18390.json`, `oxc-release-apps_v1.42.0.json`; `scratchpad/func-style-lit/main/src/ultracite/83c217ac.diff`

### 9.4 #789: TanStack route files, where no order satisfies the rule (Aug–Sep 2026). VERIFIED (issue and comments, commits, config comments); my check

- **The report** (`413n`, 29 Aug 2026, Ultracite 7.10.6, Oxlint 1.80.0): "The documented file-route pattern is mutually recursive," "so every route file that uses a route hook reports an error." "In our monorepo this is 174 findings across the route trees of four apps." The reproduction is the TanStack Router docs' pattern: `export const Route = createFileRoute(...)({ component: RouteComponent })` above `function RouteComponent() { const { partyId } = Route.useParams(); ... }`, reported as "'RouteComponent' was used before it was defined". "Declaring the component first flags its `Route` reference;" "declaring the route first flags `RouteComponent`." "Function hoisting makes the code correct at runtime either way," "but the rule still reports the textual order." The alternative, `getRouteApi("/parties/$partyId")`, "duplicates the route id as an unchecked string".
- **The docs pattern relies on hoisting.** In TanStack Router's own docs (TanStack/router at `da2adec7`, 29 Oct 2026), `path-params.md`, `routing-concepts.md` and `code-splitting.md` have 60 code blocks that define `export const Route = createFileRoute(...)`; the 42 route components in those blocks are all `function` declarations placed below the route, and none is a `const` (my count by script). Under Ultracite's `func-style` and `react/function-component-definition`, each has to become a `const` arrow and move above the route.
- **Hayden's reply** (4 Sep 2026): "agree with the direction". Three claims in it:
  - "the ESLint preset has the rule off" — true of the base rule on TypeScript files, but `@typescript-eslint/no-use-before-define` was on through the every-rule pattern (9.1) until `d9803a6e` turned it off in route files on 28 Sep 2026; on `.js`/`.jsx` files the base rule was on.
  - "Biome's `noInvalidUseBeforeDeclaration` already accepts this pattern" "(it only reports genuine temporal dead zone crashes)" — consistent with my check (9.7): Biome reported nothing on the component-first file and reported the route-first `const` version, which would crash.
  - Under Ultracite the component is an arrow, so "the component-first order is the one to use" — "with route-first, `const RouteComponent` is read before it's initialised" "and you get a real crash (TypeScript flags it as TS2448 too)." "Turning the rule off in route files is still safe" "since `tsc` catches that case."
- **The override** (`c5be8480`, 5 Sep 2026; `d9803a6e`, 28 Sep 2026), with the comment now in both TanStack presets: "File routes are mutually recursive: `Route` references the component" "via `component`, and the component calls `Route.useParams()` etc." "No declaration order satisfies the rule, and `tsc` still catches" "real temporal dead zone crashes (TS2448)." It applies to `**/routes/**/*.{js,jsx,svelte,ts,tsx}` (`ROUTE_FILE_GLOB`), which also covers React Router, Remix and SvelteKit route folders.
- **Caveat:** "`tsc` still catches" holds for this file's shape (a top-level read of a `const` declared below it in the same file). It doesn't hold for a crash reached through a helper called before the component is defined, or through an import cycle (2.13, 2.14), and "no declaration order satisfies the rule" is true only for the default options with a `const` component (2.14, 2.17). It doesn't cover `.js`/`.jsx` route files, which the glob includes and `tsc` doesn't check unless `checkJs` is on, and in those files neither linter now reports anything (9.7).
- **For the post:** the one case in Ultracite's history where the rule met real code: hoisting is what made the documented pattern work, the rule couldn't tell, and the override's safety net is the type checker. It's also the clearest case of Biome's narrower rule being the more precise one.
- **Text:** `scratchpad/func-style-lit/main/src/gh/haydenbleasel_ultracite-789.json`, `-789-comments.json`; `scratchpad/func-style-lit/main/src/tanstack/path-params.md`, `routing-concepts.md`, `code-splitting.md`; `scratchpad/func-style-lit/main/src/ultracite/c5be8480.diff`, `d9803a6e.diff`, `oxlint-tanstack-index.mjs`, `eslint-tanstack.mjs`

### 9.5 #737: an autofix turned `var` into `const` and created a TDZ crash (Jul 2026). VERIFIED (issue and comments); not about `func-style`

- **What happened:** `ultracite fix` rewrote files inside `node_modules` (the gotcha in `AGENTS.md`). Hayden's diagnosis (6 Jul 2026): "The `no-var`/`prefer-const` autofix turns TypeScript's bundled enum output" `var Comparison = ... (Comparison || {});` into the same line with `const`, "which is a self-referencing `const` — a guaranteed TDZ error at module load." Every later run then failed with "ReferenceError: Cannot access 'Comparison' before initialization". One run "rewrote **272 files inside `node_modules`**".
- **Caveat:** the files were generated code that should never have been linted; the fix was ignoring `**/node_modules`, not changing `no-var`.
- **For the post:** a concrete case of the cost the post describes: converting a hoisted binding (`var`, like a function declaration) into a TDZ binding (`const`) changes when the name can be read, and a mechanical rewrite can turn working code into a crash at load.
- **Text:** `scratchpad/func-style-lit/main/src/gh/haydenbleasel_ultracite-737.json`, `-737-comments.json`

### 9.6 What the presets set now (worktree at `7bf1b7be`, 29 Oct 2026). VERIFIED (config source; ESLint `--print-config`; my check)

- **Oxlint core** (`config/oxlint/core/index.mjs`): `func-style` `["error", "expression", { allowArrowFunctions: true }]`, `func-names` and `func-name-matching` `"error"`, `arrow-body-style` `["error", "as-needed"]`, `prefer-arrow-callback`, `no-use-before-define` (default options), `no-inner-declarations`, `no-func-assign`, `no-loop-func`, `unicorn/consistent-function-scoping`, `typescript/prefer-function-type` and `typescript/method-signature-style` `"error"`; `typescript/explicit-function-return-type` and `explicit-module-boundary-types` `"off"`. In Oxlint 1.89.0's rule list, `func-style` has fix status `pending` and `no-use-before-define` has none.
- **Oxlint React:** `react/function-component-definition` `["error", { namedComponents: "arrow-function" }]`. **TanStack:** `no-use-before-define` and `unicorn/filename-case` `"off"` in route files (9.4).
- **ESLint core** (resolved for `.ts`): `func-style` `[2, "expression", {allowArrowFunctions: true, allowTypeAnnotation: false, overrides: {}}]`, `func-names` `[2, "always", {}]`, `arrow-body-style` `[2, "as-needed"]`, `prefer-arrow-callback` `[2, {allowNamedFunctions: false, allowUnboundThis: true}]`, `unicorn/consistent-function-scoping` `[2, {checkArrowFunctions: true}]`, `no-inner-declarations` `[2, "functions", {blockScopedFunctions: "allow"}]`; base `no-use-before-define` `0` and `@typescript-eslint/no-use-before-define` `[2]` (its defaults). On `.js` the base rule is `[2, {functions: true, classes: true, variables: true, allowNamedExports: false, ...}]`. React adds `react/function-component-definition` `[2, {namedComponents: "arrow-function"}]`; TanStack turns both `no-use-before-define` rules off in route files.
- **`allowArrowFunctions` does nothing here.** In ESLint 10.13.0's `func-style.js`, arrow functions are only checked when the style is "declaration" (or `overrides.namedExports` is "declaration"); in "expression" mode the option is never consulted. See §3 for Oxlint.
- **`func-style` "expression" has two built-in exemptions:** `export default function` and a declaration with TypeScript overload signatures are never reported (ESLint source; Oxlint behaved the same in my check, 9.7).
- **But the React rule takes the first exemption back.** With the React preset, `export default function Page()` in a Next.js `app/page.tsx` and `export default function RootLayout(...)` get "Function component is not an arrow function" from both Oxlint and ESLint, with no autofix in either (my check, Oxlint core+react+next and ESLint core+react+next). The Next.js and React docs write pages and layouts that way (see §4).
- **Biome core:** `useArrowFunction`, `noInvalidUseBeforeDeclaration`, `useConsistentArrowReturn`, `noFunctionAssign`, `noInnerDeclarations` and `noArguments` `"error"`. Nothing reports a function declaration, and nothing reports a hoisted function used above its definition. The React preset's `useReactFunctionComponents` is about class components. Biome 2.6.3 does have a `func-style` port, the nursery rule `useConsistentFunctionStyle` (3.19), but the presets leave nursery rules out (`length-limits.md` 6.4). So **Biome users get no equivalent of `func-style`**, and their use-before-define rule only reports reads that would crash (9.7).
- **Text:** `scratchpad/func-style-lit/main/src/ultracite/oxlint-core-index.mjs`, `oxlint-react-index.mjs`, `oxlint-tanstack-index.mjs`, `eslint.mjs`, `eslint-typescript.mjs`, `eslint-tanstack.mjs`, `biome-core.jsonc`; `scratchpad/func-style-lit/main/check/eslint/resolved-*.json`; `scratchpad/func-style-lit/main/check/oxlint-rules.json`; `scratchpad/func-style-lit/main/check/next/oxlint-next.txt`, `eslint-next.txt`

### 9.7 What each preset reports on the same files. My check (local run, 29 Oct 2026; Oxlint 1.89.0, ESLint 10.13.0, Biome 2.6.3, Ultracite's core + react + tanstack presets unchanged)

| Case (file) | Oxlint | ESLint | Biome |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `export function add()` (`util.ts`) | `func-style` | `func-style` | nothing |
| `export default function main()` calling `helper()` declared below it | `no-use-before-define` on `helper` | `@typescript-eslint/no-use-before-define` on `helper` | nothing (hoisted, safe) |
| `function helper()` | `func-style` | `func-style` | nothing |
| overloaded `export function parse` | nothing | nothing | nothing |
| `const double = (n) => { return n * 2; }` | `arrow-body-style` | `arrow-body-style` | `useConsistentArrowReturn` (fixable) |
| route file, component first (`const Page` above `export const Route`) | nothing (override) | nothing (override) | nothing |
| route file, route first (`{ component: Page }` above `const Page`): a real TDZ crash | nothing (override) | nothing (override) | `noInvalidUseBeforeDeclaration` |
| same route-first file outside `routes/` | `no-use-before-define` | `@typescript-eslint/no-use-before-define` | `noInvalidUseBeforeDeclaration` |
| Next.js `export default function Page()` (core + react + next) | `react/function-component-definition` | `react/function-component-definition` | not run |

- **Messages:** Oxlint and ESLint: "Expected a function expression."; "'helper' was used before it was defined."; ESLint's `arrow-body-style`: "Unexpected block statement surrounding arrow body;" "move the returned value immediately after the `=>`." Biome: "This variable is used before its declaration." and "Using a variable before it is declared makes the code depend on" "declaration order and hoisting behavior."
- **One option separates the two route files:** `no-use-before-define: ["error", { variables: false }]` reports the route-first file and passes the component-first one, in Oxlint 1.89.0 and in typescript-eslint 8.72.0's version (`scratchpad/func-style-lit/main/check/variables-false/`). It ignores references from inside a function to a variable in an upper scope and keeps same-scope reads (3.4, 2.15).
- **For the post:** the two broad rules report the safe case (a hoisted call) and, in route files, are switched off for the unsafe one; Biome's narrow rule reports only the crash. Move these checks into the corpus harness before a number goes in the post.
- **Text:** `scratchpad/func-style-lit/main/check/presets/` (`src/`, `oxlint-presets.txt`, `eslint-presets.txt`, `eslint-presets.json`, `biome-presets.txt`)

### 9.8 What Ultracite tells agents about function style. VERIFIED (source)

- **The rules file** (`src/data/rules.ts`, written into AGENTS.md by `init`; mirrored in `skills/ultracite/references/code-standards.md`): "Use arrow functions for callbacks and short functions". Nothing about declarations, hoisting or order. The lint rule is broader: it reports every function declaration, long or short (9.6). The sentence dates from `18ed1f31` ("Resolves #343", 2 Nov 2025); before that the list said "Use arrow fns vs function exprs" and "Use consistent arrow fn bodies and either `interface` or `type` consistently". GitHub's Copilot docs use the same "callbacks and short functions" wording (8.5).
- **The fix prompt** (`src/agent-fix/prompt.ts`): "Make the minimal change that resolves each issue while preserving runtime behavior." and "Restyling the flagged code (renaming, re-spelling escapes, equivalent syntax) will not clear it;". For `func-style`, an equivalent-syntax rewrite is exactly the fix, and moving the definition up is the fix for `no-use-before-define`.
- **Help text an agent would read:** none beyond the messages in 9.7 for Oxlint and ESLint (see §3 for the rules' help text, if any); Biome adds "Move this use after the declaration, or move the declaration earlier."
- **Ultracite's own source follows its rule:** `packages/cli/src` (56 `.ts` files) has no function declarations and no `function` expressions by a simple grep; functions are `const` arrows.
- **For the post:** the instructions and the rule disagree on scope, and the conversion the rule asks for is the kind of restyling the prompt says won't count. An agent experiment on this rule should watch for reordering and for `this`/`arguments` breakage, since those are what make the conversion more than syntax.
- **Text:** `scratchpad/func-style-lit/main/src/ultracite/rules.ts`, `prompt.ts`, `18ed1f31.diff`

### 9.9 Ultracite's own users: one report about order, none about style. VERIFIED (issues); see 9.3, 9.4

- **Searched** (read-only `gh api search/issues` on haydenbleasel/ultracite, 29 Oct 2026): the terms listed at the top of this section. Discussions are not enabled on the repository.
- **Found:** #789 (TanStack route files, 9.4) and #551/#552 (the malformed options, 9.3). #708 (`mynameistito`, May 2026) moved a test helper to module scope "because it does not capture any variables from its parent scope" (`unicorn/consistent-function-scoping`), a change to Ultracite's own tests. #737 is the `var`-to-`const` crash (9.5). No issue asks for function declarations, complains about `func-style`, `arrow-body-style` or `prefer-arrow-callback`, or reports a crash from converting a declaration.
- **For the post:** the public record holds no complaint about the style itself; the one recorded cost is ordering.
- **Text:** `scratchpad/func-style-lit/main/src/gh/search-*.json`, `haydenbleasel_ultracite-708.json`

### 9.10 Hayden's account of why Ultracite prefers expressions (29 Oct 2026). ANECDOTAL (the maintainer's account, relayed; not in the record)

- **Source:** Hayden Bleasel, in a message relayed to the research coordinator on 29 Oct 2026, while this file was compiled; saved as `scratchpad/func-style-lit/main/src/ultracite/hayden-account-2026-10-29.txt`. No commit, issue or changelog entry says this (9.1).
- **What Hayden says:** "i preferred function expressions because they allow for dropping curly braces where appropriate" "and also i found them easier to assign types earlier on." "i also didn't super-love the concept of hoisting originally" "as i felt like it compensated for the functions being in the wrong order," "but honestly it was a personal preference."
- **The three reasons, and where the sources that bear on each are:**
  - **Concise bodies:** only arrows have them (§1); `arrow-body-style` and `useConsistentArrowReturn` then require them; research on concise versus block bodies and on lambdas and comprehension is in §5.
  - **Typing through the variable:** what a `const` annotation allows that a declaration doesn't, and the reverse (overloads, `this` parameters, assertion functions), is in §2, with TypeScript's open requests to type declarations.
  - **Hoisting as a crutch for order:** the stepdown rule and the opposite bottom-up convention, and the method-ordering research, are in §5; the spec and history of hoisting are in §1; the cost when code relies on hoisting (TanStack routes, module cycles, `var`-to-`const` rewrites) is in §6 and 9.4–9.5.
- **For the post:** state it as Ultracite's reason and as a preference, in Hayden's words. Each reason is a claim the sources can test, not a finding.

---

## Notes on Ultracite's presets (for Hayden, not for the post)

Checked in the worktree (`research/func-style`, at `7bf1b7be`) on 29 Oct 2026. Per AGENTS.md, these are for you to decide on, not to write around.

**Your account (relayed on 29 Oct 2026; not in the git history, issues or changelog)**

- **Why Ultracite prefers expressions, in your words:** "i preferred function expressions because they allow for dropping curly braces where appropriate" "and also i found them easier to assign types earlier on." "i also didn't super-love the concept of hoisting originally" "as i felt like it compensated for the functions being in the wrong order," "but honestly it was a personal preference." Recorded in 9.10. The post can state it as Ultracite's reason and as a preference; it isn't a finding.
- **Concise bodies (reason 1):** true, and specific to arrows (1.8, 1.13); `arrow-body-style` and Biome's `useConsistentArrowReturn` then require them. The one test of concise against block bodies found no significant difference (5.20). It's an argument for arrows, not for banning declarations: a codebase can have concise arrows for small functions and declarations for the rest, which is what Google's TS guide, JSLint and antfu draw the line at (4.5, 4.12, 4.19).
- **Typing through the variable (reason 2):** real. One annotation types every parameter, and TypeScript still has no way to give a declaration a function type (2.2–2.4). But other guides and rules answer it with an exception for typed variables (Google's TS guide, antfu, eslint-config-webpack, ESLint's 2025 `allowTypeAnnotation`) rather than a blanket rule (4.5, 4.12, 2.20), and several TypeScript features work better as declarations: overloads, assertion functions (TS2775 unless the variable is annotated), `this` parameters, `never` helpers, generic arrows in `.tsx` (2.5–2.9, 3.25). `React.FC`, the best-known typed-`const` pattern, fell out of favour (2.11).
- **Hoisting as a crutch for order (reason 3):** Crockford made the same argument in 2008 ("which I think leads to sloppiness.", 4.20), but the configs that act on it do so with `no-use-before-define` and declarations, not `func-style` (Airbnb's maintainer, ESLint's own codebase, webpack; 4.4, 4.16). Eich's stated purpose for hoisting was the opposite: to avoid forcing "painful bottom-up ML-like order" (1.4). And TC39 chose the TDZ so `const` functions could still be mutually recursive (1.6). The research doesn't favour either direction of file order (5.8–5.10). The post can say Ultracite chose define-before-use order on purpose, without claiming it reads better.

**Rationale**

- **No reason was recorded in the repository for `func-style`** (9.1). It has been "expression" since the first commit (Mar 2022) with no comment; V7's Oxlint value and the July 2026 ESLint value were copies. Biome-only Ultracite (Jul 2024 to Dec 2025) didn't enforce the style, and Biome users still don't.
- **The TanStack override is the only written reasoning** (9.4), and its `tsc` claim is narrower than its comment (below).

**Inconsistencies, and settings that don't do what they say**

- **`allowArrowFunctions: true` does nothing in "expression" mode,** in ESLint and Oxlint alike (3.9, 3.15, 9.6). ESLint #20689 uses this exact config as its example and is on the v11 board to make the schema reject it, so the ESLint preset may fail validation on ESLint 11. Dropping the option changes no behaviour today.
- **Mutual recursion has no compliant spelling.** With `no-use-before-define` at its defaults and `func-style` "expression", two top-level `const` functions that call each other get one error whichever comes first, though the code runs (Oxlint 1.89.0, ESLint 10.13.0; `scratchpad/func-style-lit/A/check/nubd/`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/E/check/mutual-results.txt`). The only fix is a disable comment.
- **`variables: false` would fix that and the TanStack case, at a cost.** `no-use-before-define: ["error", { variables: false }]` ignores references from inside a function to a variable in an upper scope and keeps same-scope reads. In my and the helpers' checks it passes mutual recursion and the component-first route file and still reports the route-first crash, in Oxlint 1.89.0 and in ESLint and typescript-eslint 8.72.0 (`scratchpad/func-style-lit/main/check/variables-false/`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/E/check/novars-results.txt`). Standard, love and Node.js use it (4.1). The cost: across helper B's 10 crashing cases it catches about what `tsc` catches (4 of 10), while the defaults catch 8 of 10 and also flag 5 safe cases (2.13, 2.17). So it trades call-through crash detection for no false positives on safe forward references. Worth deciding for core, and at least for route files instead of "off".
- **The TanStack route-file comment says more than `tsc` does.** "`tsc` still catches real temporal dead zone crashes (TS2448)" holds for `Route` above `const Page`; it doesn't hold for a crash through a helper called before `Page`, or through an import cycle (2.13, 2.14), and the glob also covers `.js`, `.jsx` and `.svelte` route files, which `tsc` doesn't check by default. With the rule off, neither linter reports the route-first crash; only `tsc` (TypeScript files) and Biome do (9.7). "No declaration order satisfies the rule" is a consequence of `func-style`: with a `function` component and `{ functions: false }`, the docs' own order passes (2.14). Same text in `oxlint/tanstack/index.mjs`, `eslint/tanstack/eslint.config.mjs` and `c5be8480`.
- **Your #789 reply said "the ESLint preset has the rule off".** True for the base rule on TypeScript files, but `@typescript-eslint/no-use-before-define` was on through the every-rule pattern until `d9803a6e` (28 Sep 2026), so ESLint TanStack users' route files were still reported for three more weeks (9.4). Fixed now. The 7.12.2 changelog says the ESLint change matches "the Oxlint and Biome TanStack presets"; Biome's TanStack preset has no such override (it doesn't need one).
- **`react/function-component-definition` undoes `func-style`'s default-export exemption.** `export default function Page()` and `RootLayout` in a Next.js app get "Function component is not an arrow function" from Oxlint and ESLint, with no fix (9.6). The Next.js preset doesn't relax it, so every Next.js app on Ultracite writes `const Page = () => ...; export default Page;`, unlike the React, Next.js and TanStack docs (4.17, 9.4). Worth deciding for Next.js page and layout files and React Router/Remix route modules.
- **The React rule's autofix loops.** Only `namedComponents` is set, so `unnamedComponents` keeps its default, `"function-expression"`: `export default () => <div />` is rewritten to `export default function() { ... }`, which the same rule then reports as not an arrow (unfixable) and `func-names` reports as unnamed (`scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/check/out-eslint-fix-chain.txt`, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/check/out-oxlint-fix-chain.txt`; 3.21). Setting `unnamedComponents: "arrow-function"` too would remove the loop.
- **React's own `memo` examples fail `prefer-arrow-callback`.** `const Greeting = memo(function Greeting({ name }) {` is reported in both linters; the fix turns it into an arrow and drops the explicit name. XO and love set `allowNamedFunctions: true`, which keeps named callbacks legal while still converting anonymous ones (4.1, 4.17; `scratchpad/func-style-lit/E/check/memo-results.txt`).
- **`func-names: "always"` plus `func-style` makes generators and other non-arrow functions awkward.** `function* gen()` is reported; the only compliant form is `const gen = function* gen() {}`. ESLint refused a `func-style` exception for generators in Mar 2026 because the rule is frozen (3.8, 3.9, 9.2).
- **Biome has neither half of the policy.** No `func-style` equivalent in the presets (Biome's `useConsistentFunctionStyle` is nursery, 3.19), and `noInvalidUseBeforeDeclaration` reports only reads that would crash, where Oxlint and ESLint report every out-of-order reference (3.18, 3.26, 9.7). Biome users can write declarations and forward references freely. `compare-rule-parity.ts` only compares ESLint with Oxlint, so nothing flags this. Either say so in the docs or plan to enable the nursery rule when it's promoted.
- **A silent loss waiting in the ESLint preset.** `typescript.mjs` drops deprecated typescript-eslint rules, and `eslint-typescript.mjs` turns the base `no-use-before-define` off on TypeScript files. typescript-eslint #11014 plans to deprecate its extension in favour of the core rule, which already has the TypeScript options; when that lands, TypeScript files lose the rule unless the base rule is switched back on (2.15, 2.16; `scratchpad/func-style-lit/B/check/tseslint-extension-deprecation-status.txt`).
- **Oxlint's `no-use-before-define` ran only on TypeScript files until Oxlint 1.81.0** (Sep 2026; oxc #26114). History only; the peer range is past it now (3.16).
- **What Ultracite tells agents is narrower than the rule.** `rules.ts` and `code-standards.md` say "Use arrow functions for callbacks and short functions" (since `18ed1f31`, Nov 2025); the linters reject every declaration, long or short (9.8). An agent following AGENTS.md will write declarations for longer functions and then get errors. Either the sentence or the rule should change. The rules file also says nothing about defining functions above their use.
- **The fix prompt says restyling won't clear a diagnostic**, but for `func-style` and `arrow-body-style` an equivalent-syntax rewrite is the fix (9.8). Probably harmless, but the experiment may show agents second-guessing it.
- **`arrow-body-style: "as-needed"` doesn't cover Google's concern.** Google's TS guide calls `myPromise.then((v) => console.log(v));` bad because the concise body leaks a return value; `as-needed` accepts it (4.5). Not checked for Biome's `useConsistentArrowReturn`.

**For the post and the experiment**

- **The two rules together force callees-first order,** the reverse of Clean Code's stepdown rule, which a popular community agent skill teaches (5.1, 8.11). Say plainly that Ultracite projects read callees first, and why.
- **The conversion is not mechanical.** Watch for `this`, `arguments`, `new`, `prototype`, generators, recursion, overloads and assertion functions, and above all for reordering: a declaration used above its line becomes a TDZ crash as a `const` (1.13, 1.15, 3.17, 8.12). No core tool converts declarations for this reason (3.10). A likely agent failure to test: converting `function assertIsX(v: unknown): asserts v is X` to a `const` arrow gives TS2775 at every call site (2.6). Hypothesis only.
- **Vendor example code fails the preset.** Vercel's React skill (149 declaration lines) and GitHub's Copilot example use declarations; agents primed by them and by react.dev will write declarations first (8.4, 8.5, 4.17). That's a measurable cost for the agent harness, not a reason to change the rule.
- **The runtime difference is real but small:** a `const` function called from another function carries a TDZ check in V8 bytecode, and a declaration doesn't (6.13). And esbuild turns top-level `const` into `var` when bundling, so a too-early call in production may be a `TypeError` rather than the `ReferenceError` seen in tests (6.5). Don't say the styles are identical at runtime, and don't claim `const` makes bundled code safer.
- **Wording:** Ultracite's ESLint preset turns on every non-deprecated typescript-eslint rule, close to the `all` config; don't call `no-use-before-define` a typescript-eslint recommendation (it left `recommended` in v3.0.0, 2020; 2.16, 2.18).

---

## Things that turned out wrong or different

**The language and its history, bugs and costs (sections 1 and 6)**

- **Claim: function hoisting exists because JavaScript was a rush job.** Not what Eich says. The 2011 tweet gives function hoisting a purpose (mutual recursion, avoiding bottom-up order); the 2014 tweet blames the rush job for `var` hoisting, called an unintended consequence of function hoisting (1.4). Retellings often swap the two.
- **Claim: the TDZ stops you using a binding above its line in the file.** The TDZ is about evaluation time, not position. TC39 rejected the textual rule (A1) because it would have banned mutually recursive `let`/`const` functions (1.6). `no-use-before-define` enforces something closer to the rejected rule.
- **Claim: arrow functions are anonymous, so stack traces suffer.** Since ES2015, `const f = () => {}` gets `name` `"f"` by spec, and V8 and JSC both print it in traces (1.12, 6.17). Unnamed frames come from inline callbacks and member assignments.
- **Claim: arrow functions are faster (or slower) than function declarations.** No engine-team source I found measures any difference in call speed or memory (6.16). The one measured cost is TDZ checks on `const`/`let` bindings (6.13), which affects `const` arrows and not declarations.
- **Function hoisting is what makes TDZ checks expensive, not `const` itself.** Two V8 engineers (issue 13723) and Waldemar Horwat (TC39, Oct 2024) put the difficulty of removing checks down to function declaration hoisting and forward hoisting within scopes (6.13). Claims that `let`/`const` are simply slow miss this, and JSC's 10x case was an engine bug fixed in 2020 (6.15).
- **The TDZ always protects production code.** esbuild converts top-level `let`, `const` and `class` to `var` when bundling (since 0.8.13, 2020), so an early call to a `const` arrow becomes a `TypeError` on `undefined`, not a `ReferenceError` (6.5).
- **BugsJS calls an implicit global "hoisted".** The ICST 2019 paper describes a variable used without a declaration as "hoisted" to the top of the global scope (6.9). That's implicit global creation in sloppy mode, not hoisting; don't cite it as a hoisting bug count.
- **Gallaba et al. (2015) say naming a callback keeps it from being garbage collected.** The paper gives no citation, and I found no engine source that supports it (6.12). Don't repeat it without one.
- **Ocariza et al. (2011): about 93%, not 94%.** Secondary summaries of the talk slides give 94% for the four categories; the paper says about 93% (6.6).
- **The HOPL paper's reference for the dead-zone options is titled "return when desugaring to closure".** That's the thread subject, and the options are in a reply in that thread, so the citation looks wrong but isn't (1.6).
- **MDN's Safari message may be dated.** `import-cycles.md` 2.4 quotes MDN's Safari text, `Cannot access uninitialized variable.` Bun 1.4.4's JavaScriptCore printed `Cannot access 'f' before initialization.` (with a period), matching V8. I didn't test Safari, whose JSC build may differ (1.15).
- **Spec location of the block-function legacy rules.** Older guides and the task brief point to Annex B.3.2/B.3.3 for the algorithm. In the current draft, B.3.2 keeps the rationale, but the steps are inline "Normative Optional" blocks in FunctionDeclarationInstantiation, BlockDeclarationInstantiation and GlobalDeclarationInstantiation (1.11). Cite by name.

**TypeScript (section 2)**

- **The claim that `tsc` catches temporal dead zone crashes is only half true.** It reports TS2448 when the use runs directly at the declaring level or in an immediately invoked function; it doesn't follow calls, synchronous callbacks such as `.map`, or imports. 6 of 10 crashing cases in the local test had no error (2.12, 2.13). Ultracite's TanStack comment states the strong version.
- **"No declaration order satisfies the rule" depends on the options.** It's true for `no-use-before-define` defaults with a `const` component. With `{ functions: false }` and a function declaration for the component, or `{ variables: false }` with the component first, an order satisfies the rule and runs (2.14, 2.17).
- **The CRA `React.FC` argument most often repeated is out of date.** The PR's first downside, implicit `children`, ended with `@types/react` 18.0.0, whose `FunctionComponent` takes `P` instead of `PropsWithChildren<P>` (2.11). The generics point still stands.
- **The idea that arrows can't be assertion functions is a misreading.** They can, if the variable is annotated with the assertion type; the rule is about the call target having an explicit type, and it also catches methods on objects with inferred types (2.6). The 3.7 release notes don't mention the requirement at all; only PR #32695 and #33622 do.
- **typescript-eslint's `typedefs` option does nothing under defaults.** `ignoreTypeReferences: true` takes precedence, so `typedefs: true` never reports; a maintainer said the two options conflict (#10389, open) (2.15).
- **The rule's own schema text contradicts its behaviour for `functions`.** It says "Whether to ignore references to function declarations." while `true` (the default) means report them; the `enums` text says "check" for the same polarity (2.15).
- **`no-use-before-define` isn't a typescript-eslint recommendation.** It was in `recommended` for v1 and v2 and has been only in `all` since v3.0.0 (2020) (2.16, 2.18).
- **Variance doesn't separate declarations from `const` arrows.** `strictFunctionTypes` exempts method and constructor declarations, not function declarations; `method-signature-style` is about method signatures versus function-typed properties (2.19).
- **`functions: false` doesn't help `const` code.** The option exempts only `function` declarations; `const` arrows are governed by `variables` (2.15).
- **Metadata:** typescript-eslint issue #1423's body links a later issue (#1856), so it was edited after 10 Jan 2020; quote it as the issue text, not as dated wording. GitHub shows mhegazy's association on 2017–2018 TypeScript threads as CONTRIBUTOR, though they applied the team's labels (2.4).

**The rules (section 3)**

- **ESLint's `func-style` docs describe the `var` behaviour under a `const` example.** At `v10.13.0` the docs call `doSomething()` above `const doSomething = function() {}` and say the variable is `undefined` at the time of invocation. With `const` it is uninitialized and throws `ReferenceError: Cannot access 'doSomething' before initialization`; the `undefined`/`TypeError` behaviour is `var`'s (Node 24.14.1, `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/check/out-node-semantics.txt`). The 2024 docs cleanup (PR #18477) switched the examples from `var` to `const` without changing the sentence (3.7, 3.10).
- **`allowArrowFunctions: true` is widely read as a way to prefer arrows under `"expression"`.** Biome discussion #7108 says the combination "permits arrow functions as the preferred expression style"; ESLint #20687's author and Ultracite's presets use it the same way. In ESLint and Oxlint the option is only consulted for `"declaration"` (or `namedExports: "declaration"`); with `"expression"` it changes nothing, and ESLint issue #20689 proposes rejecting the combination in v11 (3.9, 3.15).
- **`func-style` is not a hoisting-safety rule, by its author's account.** A 2016 reporter called hoisting prevention the rule's main benefit; Zakas replied that the rule is "just about style" and that the docs explain hoisting "for the edification of the reader", pointing to `no-use-before-define` for the ordering intent (#5183, 3.8).
- **Biome labels `noInvalidUseBeforeDeclaration` the `same()` as ESLint's and typescript-eslint's `no-use-before-define`, and `biome migrate eslint` maps one to the other.** Its documented valid cases (`f(); function f() {}`, a function body reading a later `const`) are errors under `no-use-before-define`'s defaults. It is closer to `no-use-before-define` with `functions`, `classes` and `variables` all `false` and `allowNamedExports: true` (3.18).
- **The claim that arrow functions are anonymous "so they produce unnamed components."** (eslint-plugin-react #690, 2016), the premise of `react/function-component-definition`, does not hold for spec-compliant engines: assigning an arrow to a `const` or a property names it (`scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/check/out-node-semantics.txt`: `inferredArrow`, `handler`); only inline callbacks and, in the 2016 Babel REPL, `export default` arrows had no name (3.21).
- **ESLint's v9 migration guide misnames `no-inner-declarations`' new option** as `blockScopeFunctions`; the rule, its docs and the codemod use `blockScopedFunctions` (3.13).
- **eslint-plugin-react's CHANGELOG lists `function-component-definition` under both 7.17.0 (28 Nov 2019) and 7.18.0 (15 Jan 2020).** PR #2414 merged on 11 Jan 2020, so 7.18.0 is the release that shipped it (3.21).
- **Oxlint's `no-use-before-define` declares `version = "1.49.0"`, but it only ran on `.js`/`.jsx` from 1.81.0** (PR #26114). Before 1.48.0 Oxlint had no such rule; Ultracite's Oxlint core enabled it on 13 Apr 2026 (3.16).
- **A `const` arrow with a `never` return type is not treated as never-returning by TypeScript.** The common assumption that annotating the return type is enough is wrong: TypeScript needs an explicit type on the variable (PR #32695; `scratchpad/func-style-lit/C/check/out-tsc-never.txt`), so this is a case where the expression style is harder, not easier, to type (3.25).
- **Biome's `useArrowFunction` fix is marked `Safe`, but it changes behaviour when the function is constructed with `new` or its `prototype` is read** (open issues #9585 and #12372; two PRs to mark it unsafe were closed unmerged) (3.17).

**Configs, guides and other languages, and what developers use (sections 4 and 7)**

- **Airbnb doesn't enforce `func-style`.** The guide's 7.1 says to use named function expressions, but `eslint-config-airbnb-base` has had `'func-style': ['off', 'expression']` with "TODO: enable" since the commit that changed the guide (7 Sep 2016) and still does at HEAD (4.2). Citing Airbnb as requiring function expressions, as a lint fact, is wrong.
- **Airbnb's 7.1 used to say the opposite.** Until Sep 2016 it read "Use function declarations instead of function expressions.", because declarations are named in stack traces (4.2). The guide's own examples kept declarations afterwards, which the maintainer put down to not cleaning them up (#1813).
- **Airbnb doesn't endorse arrows for named functions or components.** Its maintainer limits arrows to inline callbacks and says components should never be arrows (4.3). The 19.0.0 config briefly allowed only `function` expressions for components, which a user called a configuration mistake and #2501 fixed (Dec 2021).
- **_Google says use declarations_ and _Google says use arrows_ are both half right.** The TS guide prefers declarations for named functions; the JS guide's top-level examples are `const` arrows, switched from declarations in Aug 2019 with no recorded reason (4.5, 4.6). Google's lint configs enforce neither (4.7).
- **eslint-config-prettier no longer turns off `arrow-body-style` and `prefer-arrow-callback`.** Since 7.0.0 (5 Dec 2020) only `eslint-config-prettier/prettier` (via `plugin:prettier/recommended`) does; the problem is specific to running Prettier inside ESLint (4.11).
- **Deno's style guide reason is recent.** The top-level-arrow rule had no stated reason until May 2025; the section was removed on 6 May 2025 because it "induced confusion" and restored two days later (4.14). Older copies of the guide carry only the rule.
- **Unicorn's `consistent-function-style` doesn't enforce anything by default.** Issue #3 (2016) asked for a `func-style`-like rule; the rule that closed it in 2026 has every role set to `ignore` and isn't in `recommended` (4.9).
- **antfu's `top-level-function` docs omit an exemption.** The source also skips a concise arrow whose body ends on the declaration's line; the docs mention only typed variables (4.12).
- **StandardJS's 2016 plan and shipped config differ.** Aboukhadijeh planned to disallow classes used before definition; the shipped `no-use-before-define` has `classes: false` too (4.8).
- **Tómasdóttir et al.'s Table 12a percentages** for projects without a preset fit a denominator of about 3,130, not the 3,135 stated in Table 8; small, but don't recompute shares from the counts and expect the printed figures (7.3).
- **"Arrow Function Declarations" in Lucas et al. (2025) means any arrow function**, including callbacks; it isn't a count of `const f = () => {}` definitions and says nothing about declarations versus expressions (7.2).
- **Hoisting isn't needed for mutual recursion at run time.** Two `const` arrows that call each other work if called after both lines have run (`scratchpad/func-style-lit/E/check/mutual-results.txt`). What fails is `no-use-before-define` with default options, which reports one of them whichever order they're in (Oxlint 1.89.0 and ESLint 10.13.0).

**Reading order and comprehension, and agents (sections 5 and 8)**

- **Geffen and Maoz's full text is open.** `sort-keys.md` 4.9 and its dropped list say the paper's full text wasn't obtained and give its results secondhand. The author PDF is at https://www.cs.tau.ac.il/~maozs/papers/methodsorder-icpc16.pdf (linked from the TAU group page). It confirms that "Calling" means caller before callee, inspired by Clean Code. The results are more specific than "a tendency": no correctness effect, a time effect at the 25th percentile, and sensitivity among beginners only. (5.8)
- **Geffen and Maoz's corpus score flatters sparse strategies.** The denominator is all method pairs, not constrained pairs, so the finding that, "for CL, more than 79.9% of classes have received a" "score greater than 0.95." (CL is callers-first), partly reflects how few pairs that strategy constrains (3.4 distinct local calls per class on average). The authors also say IDEs that insert new methods below the caller may explain the result. Don't cite it as _most developers order callers first on purpose_. (5.8)
- **_Experts read code top to bottom_ is the opposite of the finding.** Busjahn et al.: "experts read code less linearly than novices." and follow execution order. (5.10)
- **Peitek et al.'s linearity metric doesn't measure direction**, although the prose describes calls flowing from the top of the screen to the bottom. It sums the absolute distance between call and declaration, so a callee directly above its caller scores as linear as one directly below. (5.9)
- **Torres et al.'s "arrow function" atom compared two arrows.** In the materials, the concise `x => x * x * x` is the "obfuscated" form and `(x) => { return x * x * x; }` the "clean" one. It is evidence on concise against block bodies, not arrow against `function`. (5.20)
- **Uesbeck et al. is often cited as _lambdas are harder_, but it compares lambdas with iterators** for iteration tasks, in C++, mostly with students. Professionals showed no difference. Separately, OpenAlex attaches the master's thesis abstract ("This thesis describes") and the UNLV repository location to the ICSE 2016 DOI. (5.16)
- **C doesn't force callees-first order.** Since C99 a function must be declared before use, but a prototype allows any order of definitions. The callees-first habit is a convenience, not a rule of the language. (5.3)
- **Clean Code spells the rule two ways.** The contents read "The Stepdown Rule" and the index "The Step-down Rule". (5.1)
- **"Hoist" in agent skills often means something else.** Vercel's React skill uses "Hoist Static I/O to Module Level" and "Hoist RegExp Creation" for moving work to module scope, not JavaScript declaration hoisting. Searches for "hoist" in agent guidance mostly hit this sense. (8.4)
- **"Top-down" has two meanings.** In comprehension research it means hypothesis-driven reading (Brooks; Soloway et al.), not callers-first file order, and Peitek et al. found the strategy had only a minor effect on reading order. (5.15)
- **GitHub's own Copilot example breaks the rule it states, read strictly.** It says to use arrows "for callbacks and short functions" and shows `function processData(...)` as the preferred form. Under `func-style: expression` the example's "Prefer" code is an error. (8.5)
- **Wang et al.'s "coding style inconsistencies" study has no JavaScript.** It is Python only (CoderEval), though it's sometimes cited for LLM style in general. (8.1)

**Ultracite (section 9)**

- **Ultracite didn't always enforce expressions.** The ESLint presets did from 2022 to mid-2024, but the Biome-only releases (Jul 2024 to Dec 2025) had nothing like `func-style`, and Biome users still don't (9.1, 9.6).
- **V7's Oxlint `func-style` options used a shape the rule doesn't take** (`{ "allowArrowFunctions": true, "style": "expression" }`); it surfaced only when Oxlint 1.42.0 began validating the rule's options and `ultracite fix` crashed (9.3).
- **`allowArrowFunctions: true` does nothing in Ultracite's setting**, in either linter (9.6, 3.9, 3.15).
- **Oxlint's `no-use-before-define` wasn't on from V7.** Oxlint added the rule in Feb 2026; it came on through the `restriction` category, was named in Apr 2026, and ran only on TypeScript files until Sep 2026 (9.1, 3.16).
- **`func-style` exempts `export default function`, but Ultracite's React settings report it anyway** through `react/function-component-definition`, with no fix (9.6).
- **The TanStack override's `tsc` backstop covers the direct case only** (9.4, 2.13, 2.14), and `.js`/`.jsx` route files have none.
- **Your #789 reply said the ESLint preset had the rule off;** `@typescript-eslint/no-use-before-define` was on for TypeScript route files until 28 Sep 2026 (9.4).
- **The 7.12.2 changelog says the ESLint TanStack exemption matches the Biome preset;** Biome's TanStack preset has no override, because its rule already accepts the pattern (9.4).

---

## Unverified or dropped

**The language and its history, bugs and costs (sections 1 and 6)**

- **Gyimesi et al., _BUGSJS: a benchmark and taxonomy of JavaScript bugs_, STVR 2021 (doi:10.1002/stvr.1751).** The author PDF at inf.szte.hu is 10.4 MB (over the 10 MB limit) and the Wiley full-text page returned 403. Its bug taxonomy is unchecked; 6.9 uses only the ICST 2019 paper.
- **ES2015 (6th edition) PDF.** 12.3 MB, over the limit; not needed, since the draft spec (1.9–1.15) and HOPL (1.5) cover it.
- **Hanam et al. per-pattern commit counts.** In the authors' online dataset and tech report, not in the paper text; not opened (6.8).
- **Kevin Smith's "BTF Measurements" thread** (cited on the harmony arrow page for the lexical-`this` cohort): not opened, so no numbers from it (1.8).
- **TC39 "Redmond" meeting where TDZ was agreed (about 2010).** Only the 2012 notes' reference to it ("2 years ago in redmond") and HOPL's "nearly two years"; I didn't find those minutes (1.6).
- **A large project's style-migration PR that hit TDZ errors.** `gh search prs` for `func-style`, `convert function declarations to arrow functions`, `before initialization` with arrow or hoisting, and `useArrowFunction` found none with recorded breakage. Two candidates (ant-design #58032, FranciscoMoretti/chat-js #387) were small style changes with no TDZ mention. Dropped.
- **Vite #10444, "SSR transform doesn't respect hoisting"** About `import` hoisting in Vite's SSR transform, not function style. Dropped (related to `import-cycles.md` 2.6).
- **Other bundler TDZ issues** found by `gh search issues "before initialization"` (Rollup #6064, #4101, #4187; webpack #9173; esbuild #770, #2056; swc #11045; oxc #18604; babel #15186): about class order, cycles, minifier bugs or `let`→`var` lowering, not function style. Not read in full; left out. The search results are saved in `scratchpad/func-style-lit/A/text/gh/search/`.
- **Engine posts measuring arrow vs declaration speed or memory.** Searched v8.dev, Mozilla Hacks and the WebKit blog; none found (6.16). Third-party benchmarks (SixSpeed, a 2015 incaseofstairs post) aren't engine sources and were not used.
- **Whether V8's optimising tiers remove the TDZ checks** seen in bytecode (6.13). Not measured.
- **A dedicated study of `this`-binding bugs.** A web search found none beyond Hanam et al. (6.8) and BugsJS (6.9).
- **The 2012 TDZ performance data in detail.** Hoban's 27% and 5% figures are from the email only; the Chakra prototype was never published.
- **Firefox/SpiderMonkey stack-trace and TDZ behaviour.** Not installed; not checked (6.17, 1.15).
- **The V8 design doc linked from commit `da0bcffa`** (a Google Doc): not opened.

- **typescript-eslint PR #12915** (title about detecting TDZ violations in IIFE self-references, 21 Sep 2026): closed by a bot within seconds as spam; no review. Not cited.
- **typescript-eslint.io rendered rule page:** not fetched; the `.mdx` source at `v8.72.0` was used. The site may add base-rule text the source doesn't show.

No design-meeting notes on typing declarations were found in the issue searches run (searches: `type annotation for function declaration`, `typed function declarations`, `function implements interface`, `function declaration satisfies`).

The claim that the docs' file-route pattern uses a function declaration comes from Ultracite issue #789's reporter, and the comment that `getRouteApi` "duplicates the route id as an unchecked string" was not checked. The local TanStack tests use a stub, not the library.
- **Real-code frequency of the call-through TDZ cases:** not measured; 2.13 is a probe of the checker's rule, not a sample.

**The rules (section 3)**

- **Biome discussion #3, comment 7900845** (the request that led to `noInvalidUseBeforeDeclaration`, cited in PR #1258): the saved HTML of the discussion is paginated and does not contain that comment. I used only the discussion's rule-mapping table. GitHub Discussions were not searched through the GraphQL API (to stay with read-only REST GETs); only discussions #7108 and #8963, which the Biome PRs link, were saved as HTML. There may be more discussion threads about a `func-style` equivalent.
- **The performance claims in `unicorn/consistent-function-scoping`'s docs** link a 2008 Stack Overflow answer (81329) and a Pony Foo article on V8; I did not open either, so "directly improves performance" stays the plugin's claim, not a checked fact.
- **`func-names` further reading** (Mark Daggett's 2013 post via the Wayback Machine, the 2015 2ality post on ES6 function names) and **`prefer-arrow-callback`'s MDN link**: not opened.
- **typescript-eslint issues #1236, #8488 and #10127** (earlier `func-style` requests that Josh Goldberg linked from ESLint #19719): not opened.
- **The JSHint release that shipped `latedef: "nofunc"`**: #941 says only "in the next release" (landed 25 Mar 2013); I did not map it to a version.
- **`douglascrockford/JSLint`**: the GitHub API resolves it to `jslint-org/jslint` (created 12 Nov 2010), so I used that repository's oldest `jslint.js` (2011-04-14) rather than an earlier copy. JSLint's pre-2011 history (the message's first appearance) is not on GitHub.
- **Oxlint's handling of IIFE bodies in `no-use-before-define`** (ESLint added this in 10.13.0, Oct 2026): not checked in the Oxlint source.
- **The eslint-plugin-prefer-arrow README's behaviour claims**: not checked against its source; I only read the README and npm metadata.
- **Download counts** are one week (22–28 Oct 2026) from `api.npmjs.org`; they are not trends and say nothing about which option settings people use.
- **ESLint #13261** (2020, private helpers below public functions) and **#2704, #6415, #11903** were saved but not used in entries; they repeat the requests in 3.5.
- **Who people are:** where a GitHub profile name was not confirmed by a commit author line or an earlier verified bibliography, I used the handle only (for example `camc314`, `Conaclos`, `dyc3`, `fasttime`, `gaearon`).

**Configs, guides and other languages, and what developers use (sections 4 and 7)**

- **No study compares function declarations with `const`/arrow functions**, in usage, bugs or comprehension. Searched OpenAlex (arrow functions, ES6 adoption, hoisting, coding conventions, ESLint configurations), Crossref (two queries returned results; two failed with empty responses) and arXiv (`"arrow functions"`: nothing relevant). Semantic Scholar returned HTTP 429 for three of four queries.
- **State of JS:** no question on function style in any edition 2016–2026 (question files grepped); arrows only as a feature-awareness item in 2019–2020 (7.1). I took 2019's numbers from the 2020 results file, not the 2019 site (its page-data URL served HTML).
- **"TypeScript’s Evolution: An Analysis of Feature Adoption Over Time" (MSR 2023, 10.1109/msr59073.2023.00027):** title seen in OpenAlex only; not opened (TypeScript features, unlikely to cover function style).
- **Depaz (RESET 2022)** opened via Wayback; it doesn't discuss `func-style` or hoisting, so it's used only for its general claim (7.10).
- **Google styleguide PR #464** has no description beyond "See issue #462"; I didn't open #462, so the reason for the 2019 JS-guide change is unknown (4.6).
- **_JavaScript: The Good Parts_:** only O'Reilly's preview of Appendix B's function-statement section (Wayback, 26 Nov 2022) was opened. The live O'Reilly page needs a login; a 2025 Wayback capture was the section on block-less statements, not this one.
- **Springer PDF for Lucas et al.** returned an HTML page; used the Universidade do Minho repository PDF instead (marked submitted version by OpenAlex, though it carries the journal header).
- **OpenEdition (Depaz)** served a bot-check page to curl; used the Wayback capture of 5 Jul 2025.
- **JSLint's current help:** the master `index.html` (2026.9.29) has no help text; used the archived 2014 and 2020 website branches.
- **Not traced:** when Airbnb set `prefer-arrow-callback`; when ESLint's own config adopted `func-style: declaration`; the commit that gave eslint-config-standard its current `no-use-before-define` options; when Node.js switched `no-use-before-define` options after 2017.
- **Svelte, Vue, Babel, Prettier, TypeScript, React:** searched issues for function-style discussions (`scratchpad/func-style-lit/E/text/projects-search.txt`); nothing relevant beyond lint settings and contributing guides (7.8, 7.9). `gh search` on facebook/react returned HTTP 422. microsoft/TypeScript's main branch is now the Go port and has no ESLint config.
- **eslint-config-next** not checked for these rules.
- **C23 draft (N3220)** not opened; N1256 (C99) covers the removal of implicit declarations.
- **Haskell top-level declaration order** not checked (only §3.12 `let` read).
- **Code counts of which style big codebases use** (beyond Node.js's 2017 commit message and my grep of two docs sites) left for the `research/` corpus harness.

**Reading order and comprehension, and agents (sections 5 and 8)**

- **Clean Code Chapters 3 and 5, verbatim:** not opened. The Pearson sample has the front matter, Chapter 1 and the index. The Wayback captures of Safari Books Online (2011–2014) show only each section's first paragraph, and the O'Reilly captures (2019) only each chapter's opening. No legitimate copy of the stepdown or newspaper text was found. Only the downward-dependency sentence, as quoted by Geffen and Maoz, is used. Web summaries (DZone, Stack Overflow, Goodreads notes, agent skills) were not used.
- **K&R, _The C Programming Language_:** no legitimate open copy. The C99 committee draft (N1256) was used instead.
- **Wirth's 1973 Pascal report on `forward`:** the directive isn't in the 1973 Revised Report (searched the OCR text). The Jensen–Wirth _User Manual and Report_ scan on archive.org was not used (copyright status unclear). ISO 7185 was read from the unofficial HTML copy on standardpascal.org.
- **Peachock et al., HCII 2017:** Springer full text not fetched. It is cited only through Peitek et al.'s summary (5.11, UNVERIFIED).
- **Brooks 1983 and Pennington 1987:** abstracts elided in OpenAlex and Semantic Scholar, and the full texts are Elsevier-only. Metadata only. Semantic Scholar's response pointed to an Unpaywall URL, which was not used.
- **Uesbeck et al., ICSE 2016 paper:** ACM, not opened. The thesis on the same experiment was read via Wayback (the live UNLV repository returned a Cloudflare challenge). One Wayback capture under the `rtds` context served a different thesis (Wu 2007) and was discarded.
- **Lucas et al., SBES 2019:** ACM, not opened. The JSERD 2020 extension was used.
- **Petrulio, Sawant and Bacchelli, _The indolent lambdification of Java_ (EMSE 2021):** Springer and ZORA returned challenge pages, and it's a usage study, so it was dropped.
- **Alves, Oliveira, Madeiral and Castor, "On the Bug-proneness of Structures Inspired by Functional Programming in JavaScript Projects" (arXiv:2206.08849):** read, then dropped. It counts "functions as values" and thunks (arrows, function expressions) together and doesn't separate declaration from expression. Its bug-fix result (such structures are removed less often in bug-fixing commits) says nothing about function form.
- **_Naming anonymous JavaScript functions_ (OOPSLA companion 2011) and "Understanding the adoption of modern JavaScript features" (EMSE 2025):** not opened. They are about stack-trace naming and adoption, which other helpers cover.
- **_code_transformed_ (arXiv:2506.12014 v2):** read, then dropped. It covers Python naming and complexity trends only, with no JavaScript.
- **IFEvalCode (arXiv:2507.22462 v2):** read, then dropped. It has JavaScript and TypeScript tasks with style constraints, but "arrow" doesn't appear in the text.
- **MultiAIGCD (arXiv:2507.21693):** not opened beyond a search snippet. It reports function presence, not function form.
- **Paltoglou et al., arXiv:2107.10164:** about refactoring to ES6 modules, not arrows. Not opened.
- **A study measuring arrow against declaration in LLM output, an agent converting declarations to arrows, or TDZ breakage introduced by agents:** searched (OpenAlex, web, `gh search issues` in anthropics/claude-code and openai/codex) and nothing found.
- **"Learning Program Component Order" (2017) and other papers citing Geffen and Maoz:** listed by OpenAlex and Semantic Scholar but not opened, because their titles are about code review or experiment design. Baum et al. are covered in `sort-keys.md` 4.10–4.11.
- **The Cursor docs `.md` URL** returned "Page not found". The HTML page was used.
- **Springer article pages and PDFs** returned a Client Challenge page to curl. The Wayback captures were used instead.

**Ultracite (section 9)**

- **Why `func-style` was set to "expression" in 2022:** no commit, issue or changelog entry; only your account (9.10). Discord, X and other channels weren't searched; GitHub Discussions aren't enabled on the repository.
- **What Oxlint did with V7's malformed `func-style` options before 1.42.0:** not checked; "expression" is the default either way (9.3).
- **How often `func-style` and `no-use-before-define` fire in real code, and what converting a codebase costs:** left to the corpus harness (`research/corpus/results/func-style/`); nothing here depends on it.
